# Gap Register — autonomous improvement log

Running log of source-intelligence improvements, one line per change: **what / why
(LE-defence evidentiary or OSINT-quality value) / test result**. Newest at the top.

> **Baseline note (honest reconciliation).** The autonomous protocol that seeded this
> loop stated a CURRENT STATE of `v9.0.0 · 8,239 LOC · 29 modules · 46 tests` and an
> `LlmAuditRecord` priority. That describes a different codebase. This repository is
> **v1.9.0 · ~172k LOC · 124 modules · 3,539 lib + integration tests**, and it carries a
> **guard-enforced no-AI/ML/LLM invariant** (`runtime_carries_no_ai_ml_inference_dependency`),
> so there are no runtime LLM call boundaries to audit — that priority is inapplicable and
> was not built (it would be a dead field, failing this register's own quality bar). The
> loop is therefore run against the **real** baseline, on genuine improvements, keeping the
> actual 3,539-test suite green.

| 2026-07-14 | **Built the movement/path reconstruction layer over `LocationVisited` timeline events — the increment the same day's earlier `exif_geo`/`shot_time` fix had explicitly named as "now buildable." Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` C5 (movement-path leg) / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-GEOINT (movement-path leg).** New pure `core::timeline::movement_path(&[TimelineEvent]) -> Option<Movement>` walks a reconstructed timeline's dated `LocationVisited` fixes (currently sourced from `exif_geo`'s decoded EXIF capture instants) in chronological order, parses each fix's coordinate value via the existing universal parser (`util::geo::coords::parse`, already allow-listed for `core` use — no new layering exception needed), and sums the canonical `util::geo::haversine_km` great-circle distance between each consecutive pair. Returns `None` below 2 parseable fixes (a single dated photo is a point, not a path — reporting one would misstate the evidence as a "movement" that wasn't observed), and skips any classified-but-unparseable fix defensively rather than breaking the chain into disjoint halves. Wired to both output surfaces: a new `MOVEMENT` CLI dossier section (only printed when a real path exists, directly below `TIMELINE`) and an additive `movement` field on `GET /api/v1/scans/{id}/timeline` (`null` when absent — the existing response shape is otherwise unchanged), with SPA parity in `scan_info/timeline.js`'s new "Movement path" panel reusing the `location_visited` colour the prior increment introduced. [new-capability, geoint-differentiator, evidentiary-integrity, scope-discipline] | A single dated GPS-tagged photo tells an analyst where a subject was once; two or more, walked chronologically with real distances between them, tell a materially different story — the beginnings of an actual movement pattern, a capability neither SpiderFoot nor Maltego offers on-device. Refusing to synthesise a "path" from fewer than 2 fixes (rather than always emitting a possibly-empty or single-point structure) keeps the feature honest: it only ever asserts a movement the evidence actually supports. | 4635 lib tests (+3): `movement_path_none_with_fewer_than_two_fixes` pins the 0/1-fix `None` case; `movement_path_walks_real_fixes_chronologically_with_real_distance` uses a real NYC↔Sydney pair (asserting the real-world ~15,990 km great-circle distance to ±200 km, and that chronological ordering doesn't depend on caller-supplied event order); `movement_path_sums_multiple_legs_and_skips_unparseable_fixes` (real Brisbane↔Sydney ~730 km) proves an unparseable fix is skipped without breaking the leg chain. Since `movement_path` is wholly new code, `git stash` gives the strongest possible regression signal — confirmed the pre-fix tree has zero matching tests to even compile against. Live-verified against the real compiled binary: `hse selftest` 9/9; a real `hse scan -k domain -v github.com -m dns_intel` dossier correctly omits the `MOVEMENT` section entirely (no dated location fixes on a DNS-only scan, no crash); the same scan's live `/api/v1/scans/{id}/timeline` response (via `hse serve`) confirmed `"movement": null` alongside the unchanged pre-existing fields. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |
| 2026-07-14 | **`gravatar::Account::verified` was typed `Option<String>` for a "Gravatar serialises this as the string true/false" assumption that no longer holds — the live API sends a genuine JSON boolean, so `Account`'s (and, since it nests inside `Entry` inside `GravatarResp`, the WHOLE profile's) deserialization silently failed on every real hit with a linked social account. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.101 (new) / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-GRAVATAR-VERIFIED-BOOL (new).** A project-wide (not same-file-only, which produces false positives when a struct's consumer lives in a sibling file) sweep for the T2.97-100 "parsed but never read" class flagged `verified` as apparently unread from `types.rs`-style inspection; live investigation found something worse than dead code — a genuine parse failure. Fetching Gravatar's own documented example profile live (`gravatar.com/205e460b479e2e5b48aec07710c08d50.json`) returned `"accounts":[{"verified":true,…}]`, a bare JSON boolean; `serde_json` rejects that against `Option<String>` with `invalid type: boolean true, expected a string`, and `process()` folds ANY parse error into the identical "no Gravatar profile" empty result it uses for a genuine 404 (`Ok(None) | Err(_) => return Ok(result)`) — so a real hit with linked accounts was silently indistinguishable from a real miss. Fix: `deserialize_flexible_bool`, a `#[serde(deserialize_with)]` helper accepting either a bool or the original string and normalising both to `Option<bool>` — additive, doesn't regress the older API generation. [silent-false-negative-fix, evidentiary-integrity, real-data-validated, live-verified-both-sides] | A profile the module previously reported as "no Gravatar" was in fact a REAL hit carrying a self-asserted name, location, avatar, and verified links to the subject's Twitter/LinkedIn/GitHub/Instagram accounts — exactly the identity-graph enrichment this module exists to surface, silently lost to a type mismatch rather than a genuine absence. This is a strictly worse failure mode than T2.97-99's single-field drops: those degraded one evidentiary fact per record, this one dropped the entire record, silently, on what is very likely the majority of real Gravatar profiles today (any with ≥1 linked account). | 4632 lib tests (+2): `real_profile_response_with_boolean_verified_parses_and_yields_entities` runs the exact live JSON shape through `extract_entry` end-to-end, confirmed via `git stash` to fail to compile against the pre-fix `Option<String>` typing; `account_verified_accepts_legacy_string_shape_too` pins that the original string shape still works, so the fix is additive. LIVE-VERIFIED on both sides of the fix against the real binary: `hse scan -k email -v beau@automattic.com -m gravatar` (a public Automattic employee's own self-published Gravatar profile) returned 0 gravatar entities pre-fix, 13 post-fix (name, 2 username pivots, 5 URLs incl. 4 verified social accounts, an address), newly firing 8 correlator rules (AU-018/067/076/034/060/070/101/063) that never fired before. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |
| 2026-07-14 | **Closed F.3's `cargo-fuzz` leg — the last fully-unstarted item in the §3.F proof-infrastructure foundation. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` F.3 (cargo-fuzz leg) / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-F3 (cargo-fuzz leg).** Picked up this standing in-progress node per step 1 of the cycle protocol rather than opening something new — F.3's own text had named `cargo-fuzz` as the sole remaining item since the 2026-06-17 import-parser proptest cycle. New `fuzz/` crate (`cargo fuzz init` layout, deliberately outside the root `Cargo.toml`'s package — it has no `[workspace]` table — since `libfuzzer-sys` needs nightly-only sanitizer instrumentation that would fail on the stable toolchain the four-command gate runs on) targets `cert_intel`'s hand-rolled DER scanner: the highest-value untrusted-byte parser in the codebase, and the same code T2.3's *fixture* testing alone already found two real bugs in (a SAN `OCTET STRING → SEQUENCE` unwrap miss, a serial/version INTEGER mix-up). New `#[doc(hidden)] pub fn fuzz_entry_parse_der` calls the three previously-private DER extractors so one target covers the whole scanner — a single, documented, harness-only visibility widening, safe since the crate is `publish = false` and never consumed as a published API. New advisory-quality `.github/workflows/fuzz.yml` (mirrors `audit.yml`: weekly + on-demand + on a relevant path change, NOT a required check) seeds a scratch `fuzz/corpus/cert_der/` from the existing `cert_intel/testdata/selfsigned.der` fixture and runs a bounded 120s fuzz-and-replay pass. **Found and fixed a real bug in this cycle's own tooling before it shipped**, not in Huntsman: libFuzzer treats every corpus directory on its command line as read *and write*, so the first local invocation (pointed directly at `src/modules/cert_intel/testdata/`) silently wrote 574 newly-discovered files into the real fixture directory; caught via `git status` before committing, confirmed all 574 were untracked byproducts (the one tracked `selfsigned.der` was untouched), removed with a scoped `git clean -fdx`, and both the local command and the CI workflow fixed to seed a dedicated gitignored corpus dir instead. [proof-infrastructure, coverage-guided-fuzzing, foundations-tier, self-caught-tooling-bug] | Coverage-guided fuzzing (libFuzzer's mutation engine, guided by real code coverage) reaches states random `proptest` generation over a fixed byte-length range does not — genuinely deeper assurance against a remote-DoS panic in a parser that runs on fully attacker-controlled bytes from a live TLS peer, exactly the class of untrusted input this evidentiary tool's own doctrine (T2.3, T0-class boundary safety) treats as security-relevant, not just a robustness nicety. | No production code change beyond the one `#[doc(hidden)]` visibility widening (dev/build-only, zero runtime cost); the stable-toolchain gate (fmt/clippy/doc/test) never touches `fuzz/`, which sits outside the root package entirely — full suite unchanged, gate green. Live-verified for real, not just scaffolded: a session-installed nightly toolchain + `cargo-fuzz` built the harness (11m57s, whole-crate ASAN rebuild) and ran it — 1,243,784 executions against the real seed in 60 seconds, 0 crashes. |
| 2026-07-14 | **`netlas::NetlasResp::count` looked like a fourth T2.97-99 dropped-evidentiary-field instance (parsed, never read) but investigation found it isn't one — dead code, not dropped evidence. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.100 (new) / `SOLUTION_TREE` §4a note (new).** The same grep sweep that found T2.97-99 flagged `count: Option<u64>` on `NetlasResp` too. Unlike those three, checking Netlas's own API docs (two independent fetches of `docs.netlas.io`) shows `GET /api/responses/` — the exact endpoint this module calls — never returns a match-total field; the total lives only on a separate `GET /api/responses_count/` endpoint this module doesn't call, so `count` was always `None` against the real API. Fix: removed the dead field (1 line), with a doc comment recording the investigation so a future sweep doesn't re-flag the same false lead. Deliberately did not add a second `responses_count/` call to manufacture a real total — that's a real per-scan quota-cost increase on the operator's key, out of scope for a mechanical cleanup. [dead-code-cleanup, investigation-only, scope-discipline] | Building T2.99-style `total_matches` disclosure logic around a field that never carries real data would have fabricated a finding — the same hard constraint against fabricating findings in entity/evidence output applies to the investigation process that decides whether a "gap" is real. Recording the negative result (like `PROBLEM_TREE` §6's other verified-sound entries) stops a future pass re-discovering and re-investigating the same false lead. | No test relied on the removed field (confirmed by grep: every existing `netlas` test constructs `NetlasResp` bodies without a `count` key already); full suite unchanged (4630 lib tests). Gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |
| 2026-07-14 | **`psbdmp::SearchResp::count` — the psbdmp.ws API's own authoritative paste-match total — was parsed but silently discarded, the third dropped-evidentiary-field instance this arc. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.99 (new) / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-PSBDMP-TOTAL-MATCHES (new).** Confirmed by grep: `count` is deserialised into `SearchResp` and never read anywhere else in the module — the same class as T2.97 (`stealer::Cred::pwned_at`) and T2.98 (`structured_id`'s 3 missing `AccountCreated` keys), found via the same heuristic sweep (a `#[derive(Deserialize)]` field parsed but never read) that surfaced both of those. Only the locally deduped `data.len()` (`paste_count`) ever reached evidence; when the server reports more matches than a given response's `data` array actually carried (upstream pagination/truncation), the subject's real paste exposure was silently understated rather than disclosed. Fix: added a `total_matches` evidence attribute — `(resp.count as usize).max(paste_count)` — set only when it exceeds `paste_count`, folded into the summary text (`"... ({total_matches} reported by source)"`); deliberately never replaces or shrinks `paste_count`, never fabricates URLs for the undisclosed matches. [dropped-field-fix, evidentiary-integrity, systematic-sweep, scope-discipline] | Silently discarding a real, parsed field the source API itself supplies is the same "looks complete but isn't" failure this project's doctrine treats seriously — an analyst reading `paste_count` as the subject's full paste exposure would be unaware the source claims more matches exist. Disclosing the gap (rather than fabricating URLs for it, or silently keeping the undercount) matches this codebase's standing evidentiary-honesty precedent (T2.94's "the web path does MORE... name it honestly" framing, applied here to an API response instead of UI copy). | 4630 lib tests (+2): `extract_surfaces_total_matches_when_server_count_exceeds_shown_pastes` pins the disclosure (server `count:5` vs. 2 shown pastes → `total_matches:5` + summary text), git-stash-proven to fail (`None`) pre-fix and pass (`Some("5")`) post-fix; `extract_never_understates_total_matches_below_pastes_actually_shown` guards the inverse (a `count` at/below `paste_count` must never add or shrink anything); the existing equal-count test gained an explicit no-`total_matches` assertion. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |
| 2026-07-14 | **`core::timeline::classify`'s `AccountCreated` fix (2026-07-05, C1(c)) only wired 1 of `structured_id`'s 4 timestamp-embedding ID decoders — `objectid_created_date`/`ulid_created_date`/`ksuid_created_date` stayed silently absent from the footprint timeline. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.98 (new) / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-STRUCTURED-ID-TIMELINE (new).** Ran a systematic sweep for the same dead-signal class the `shot_time`/`pwned_at` fixes earlier this session closed: grepped every date-shaped evidence-attribute key literal across `src/modules` against `classify`'s recognised set. Fix: added the three keys to the existing `AccountCreated` match arm — 3 lines, no new `TimelineEventKind`, no parser change, since the value shape `parse_date` already accepts is identical. [dead-signal-fix, evidentiary-integrity, real-data-validated, scope-discipline, systematic-sweep] | A decoded ID's embedded creation timestamp is direct evidence — exactly the "AccountCreated" fact class the 2026-07-05 fix set out to stop silently dropping — and three of the four decoders that produce it were still being dropped after that fix shipped, an easy thing to miss since the match arm LOOKS complete (it has an `AccountCreated` case) while actually only covering 1 of 4 live producers. The same discipline that found `shot_time`/`pwned_at` this session (grep the real evidence-attribute keyspace against the classifier's recognised set, rather than assume a prior fix was exhaustive) is what surfaced this one, reinforcing it as a standing, repeatable check rather than a one-off. | 4628 lib tests (+1): the existing `classify_maps_every_live_account_created_key_not_leaving_it_dead_code` test extended to cover all 8 `AccountCreated` keys (was 5); new `reconstruct_surfaces_a_structured_id_ulid_created_event_end_to_end` proves the full pipeline, git-stash-proven to fail (0 events) pre-fix and pass (1 `AccountCreated` event) post-fix. Live-verified twice against the real compiled binary: `hse scan --kind username --value 01ARZ3NDEKTSV4RRFFQ69G5FAV --modules structured_id --depth 0 -o dossier` (a real ULID) now shows a `2016-07-30 account_created` timeline event where before the fix TIMELINE was empty; a second real run with a MongoDB ObjectID (`507f1f77bcf86cd799439011`) shows the equivalent `2012-10-17 account_created` event via `objectid_created_date`. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |
| 2026-07-14 | **`cli/import/stealer.rs`'s `Cred::pwned_at` field — parsed from a real, module-documented `Pwned At:` line in the Stealerlogs victim-export format — was written once and read nowhere else in the codebase, silently discarding a specific credential's own compromise date on every stealer-log import. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.97 (new) / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-STEALER-PWNED-AT (new).** Confirmed by grepping every `.pwned_at` access in `src/`: exactly one hit, the assignment itself (`stealer.rs:168`) — no consumer anywhere. This is a full-fidelity-policy gap, not a missing timeline classification: `Evidence`'s own doc comment states the operator policy directly ("the FULL source record, preserved verbatim for traceability... nothing redacted or omitted, credentials included"). Investigated whether the fix should ALSO wire `pwned_at` (and the already-live victim-level `newest`/`oldest` dates) into `core::timeline::classify` and deliberately did not: the C1(c) 2026-07-05 log entry established that `classify()` is scoped to first-party scan-MODULE evidence only, precisely so the footprint timeline never fires reconstructed events from arbitrary `cli/import` bulk-dump data — confirmed by grep that no `cli/import/*.rs` parser emits any currently-recognised timeline key today, so widening it here would be a first, unjustified crossing of that boundary rather than a mechanical follow-on to the `shot_time` fix immediately above. Fix, scoped to the real gap: each credential's `pwned_at` (when present) now rides as a `pwned_at` evidence attribute on ONLY the entities that credential itself yields — its `Email`/`Username`, and its `Credential` when a plaintext password is present — not on the shared victim-level evidence cloned onto the machine/domain entities, since the date is per-credential, not per-victim. `stealer.rs`'s `push` closure took an explicit `Evidence` parameter (previously an implicit `ev.clone()`) so the two call-site families can diverge correctly. [dropped-field-fix, evidentiary-integrity, real-data-validated, scope-discipline] | A credential's own compromise date is a distinct, useful evidentiary fact from the victim-level log-date range Huntsman already surfaces — it tells an analyst exactly when THIS specific account was exposed, not just when the log as a whole was captured. Silently discarding a real, parsed field an operator's own imported data supplies is the same "looks complete but isn't" failure this project's doctrine treats seriously even when (as here) nothing false was reported — data was omitted, not fabricated, but every full-fidelity guarantee this codebase makes elsewhere (the `Evidence` struct's own doc, the T2.7 honest-failure precedent) exists to prevent exactly this kind of quiet loss. | 4627 lib tests (+1): `stealerlogs_credential_pwned_at_survives_onto_its_own_entities` pins that a real capture's `Pwned At:` date reaches its entity's evidence, git-stash-proven to fail (`None`) pre-fix and pass (the real ISO instant) post-fix. Live-verified: a real `hse import <file>` run against a genuine Stealerlogs-shaped body shows `"pwned_at": "2026-05-20T21:00:00Z"` in the JSON output on both entities the sample credential yields. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |
| 2026-07-14 | **`exif_geo`'s real `shot_time` evidence (a photo's EXIF capture instant, stamped on every entity it emits including the geolocated `Coordinates` entity) was silently invisible to the footprint timeline — the "movement/timeline geo" capability `PROBLEM_TREE` C5 has named as remaining since cycle 19 turned out to already have a live data source with nothing consuming it. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` C5 (first increment) / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-GEOINT (extended), paired with the C1(c) timeline-widening precedent.** Confirmed live in the source, not assumed: `exif_geo::process` (`src/modules/exif_geo/mod.rs`) reads `Tag::DateTimeOriginal`/`Tag::DateTime` and folds the value onto the shared per-entity evidence as `"shot_time"` unconditionally whenever either tag is present in a fetched image's EXIF — already-shipped, already-running code. `core::timeline::classify` had no arm for the key at all, so it was dropped exactly like `TimelineEventKind::AccountCreated` was before the 2026-07-05 fix (same defect class, this time on a geo-typed key rather than an identity one). A second, independently-found defect made it a two-part fix: `exif_geo::parse::read_str` returns the tag's ASCII value verbatim in EXIF's own format (`"YYYY:MM:DD HH:MM:SS"`, colon-separated date, not ISO), but `timeline::parse_date` only recognised `-`/`/` as date separators — so a correctly-classified `shot_time` would still have silently failed to parse. Fix: added `TimelineEventKind::LocationVisited` (kept distinct from the existing `Generic` bucket — a dated place is a materially more useful, differently-shaped fact than an unclassified date) and mapped `"shot_time"` to it in `classify()`; extended `parse_date`'s separator detection to accept `:` as a third option, safe because the date portion is already isolated from any `HH:MM:SS` time component by the function's own leading `split_once(['T', ' '])` before separator detection ever runs — no ambiguity with the time's own colons. SPA parity: `src/web/js/scan_info/timeline.js`'s `TL_KIND` map gained a `location_visited` entry (map-marker icon, its own colour) so the new kind renders with a real label instead of silently falling through to the generic "Event" badge. Deliberately scoped to `exif_geo` only: audited every other module that produces `Coordinates` entities (WiGLE, `cell_intel`, `opencellid`, the IP-geo family, `address_au`) for a similar per-observation timestamp evidence attribute — none carry one today (confirmed by grep, not assumed), so `shot_time` is genuinely the only live dated-geo signal in the codebase right now; a broader multi-event movement/path-reconstruction layer stays a real, separate future increment — not invented ahead of the data that would justify it. [dead-signal-fix, evidentiary-integrity, real-data-validated, scope-discipline, capability-increment] | A dated GPS coordinate is direct physical-presence evidence — exactly the kind of fact an evidentiary OSINT tool exists to surface, not silently drop. Before this fix, an operator investigating a subject whose photo carried EXIF GPS + a capture date would see the `Coordinates` entity itself (via the entity table/GEXF) but never see it dated on the footprint timeline, understating the subject's documented movement history without any indication anything was missing — the same "looks complete but isn't" failure mode this project's evidentiary doctrine treats as seriously as a false positive. Fixing the classification without also fixing the date-format gap would have been a half-fix that still silently dropped every real value; finding and closing both in one commit avoids shipping a "fix" that doesn't actually work against the real data shape it targets. | 4626 lib tests (+3): `classify_recognises_exif_shot_time_as_location_visited` pins the classification; `parse_date_accepts_the_real_exif_datetime_format` pins the EXIF-format date parse against the exact real tag shape; `reconstruct_surfaces_a_location_visited_event_from_a_real_exif_shot_time` proves the full pipeline end-to-end against a `Coordinates` entity carrying `exif_geo`'s real evidence shape. Git-stash-provable: the pre-fix `classify` has no `shot_time` arm at all, so the new tests fail to zero events pre-fix and pass post-fix. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |
| 2026-07-14 | **Golden-fixture corpus, seventh slice: you.com — disproved its own stale doc comment and fixed a real `ENGINE_DOMAINS` chrome-leak on the engine's own CDN domain. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.7 (seventh slice) / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-HEALTH-SIGNAL (extended).** Swept live reachability of all 10 remaining un-fixtured `search_engines` engines from this sandbox first: `yahoo` (HTTP 500), `aol` (404), `mojeek` (403), `yandex`/`qwant` (302 redirect), `ecosia`/`presearch` (403), `searx` (503) — 7 confirmed blocked/unreachable at the HTTP layer, matching each engine's own documented live-scan stats; `google` returned `200` but with a genuine "having trouble accessing Google Search" JS-challenge interstitial (already correctly caught by the existing `is_captcha_page` detector — no fix needed there). Only `you` returned `200` with usable content. Fetched a REAL live `you.com` response for `GET /search?q=Kylo4kylo&tbm=youchat` (exactly `EngineSpec::build_url`) and checked it in verbatim as `fetch/testdata/you_kylo4kylo.html` (55 KB, unmodified). This capture disproved `engines.rs`'s own doc comment, which claimed you.com "exposes a classic /search HTML view with referrer-style result anchors" — the real page is a Cloudflare-gated Next.js SPA (`__NEXT_DATA__` JSON payload only) with ZERO `<a href="…">` result anchors anywhere in the body; every genuine result is hydrated client-side by JS this engine never executes. The Cloudflare challenge loader (`/cdn-cgi/challenge-platform/…`) is present verbatim, matching an existing `BLOCK_VENDOR_SIGNATURES` fingerprint, so `is_captcha_page` already correctly classifies this specimen `Blocked`. It also surfaced a real, separate chrome-leak defect: the generic href-extraction pass reads ANY `href=` attribute, not just `<a>` result anchors, and this capture's own `<link rel="dns-prefetch" href="https://cdn.you.com"/>` tag leaked through `parse_results` as a single fake organic hit because `you.com` was never in `ENGINE_DOMAINS` (only its sibling engines' domains were) — the same false-positive class already fixed for MetaGer/Dogpile/Swisscows/Startpage. Fixed by adding `you.com` to `ENGINE_DOMAINS` (`helpers/urls.rs`); corrected the stale `engines.rs` doc comment to record the confirmed Cloudflare-gated-SPA shape instead of the disproven "classic HTML view" claim. [false-positive-fix, evidentiary-integrity, real-data-validated, honest-disclosure, golden-fixture-corpus] | A stale doc comment claiming a working classic HTML scrape path for an engine that is actually a JS-only SPA would mislead a future maintainer into debugging the wrong layer; correcting it, backed by a real capture, keeps the module's self-description trustworthy. The chrome-leak fix matters independently of the block classification: even on an engine correctly flagged `Blocked` today, a future relaxation of `is_captcha_page` (or a Cloudflare-bypass) would otherwise silently attribute you.com's own CDN link to the scan subject as a fake organic result — closing it now means the engine is honest on both axes (block detection AND result extraction), not just the one currently exercised. | 4623 lib tests (+2): `is_captcha_page_detects_a_real_youcom_cloudflare_challenge_capture` pins the block classification against the real capture; `parse_results_excludes_youcoms_own_cdn_chrome` pins the `ENGINE_DOMAINS` fix. Git-stash-proven: reverting the `you.com` `ENGINE_DOMAINS` addition alone reproduces the leak (`https://cdn.you.com` reappears as the sole fake "result"); restored, it passes. All 306 pre-existing `search_engines`-scoped tests (304 before this slice's 2 additions) continue to pass unchanged. LIVE-VERIFIED: a real `hse scan --kind name --value Kylo4kylo --modules search_engines --depth 0` run reports `engine: you, outcome: blocked, results: 0`, with zero `cdn.you.com` (or any other you.com-chrome) entity anywhere in the scan's entity table. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |
| 2026-07-14 | **`au_property`'s three government portal legs (NSW ELVIS, VIC MapShare WFS, QLD titles search) are all now confirmed DEAD via real live requests — reachable, not proxy-blocked, and every one returns a genuine `404` — but `process()` silently swallowed every failure into `Ok(ModuleResult::new())`, indistinguishable from "no property record for this name." Closes the T2.7 remaining item's stale "still proxy-blocked" note for this module and fixes the silent-failure defect. Paired `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.7 / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-HEALTH-SIGNAL (extended).** Live-confirmed with real requests, no fixture: NSW's `maps.six.nsw.gov.au/services/public/Property_Name_Address` → `404` (the root domain now serves an unrelated client-rendered "SDT Explorer" SPA at `/explorer/`, confirmed by fetching the live homepage — the same "legacy static endpoint retired for a client-rendered app" pattern already confirmed for `au_electoral`'s AEC leg and `metager`); VIC's `mapsharevic/ows` WFS endpoint → `404` (a real IIS "File or directory not found" page; the root MapShareVic app itself still `200`s, so this is the specific API path, not the domain); QLD's `environment/land/title/searching/owners` → `404` (qld.gov.au's own genuine 178&nbsp;KB "Page not found" template). Distinguished from a proxy/connectivity failure by confirming each root domain is separately reachable (`200`/`200`) — the identical class of evidence this project has used for every prior "legacy endpoint retired" finding (`au_electoral`, `au_people`, `metager`, `domainsdb`, `huggingface_user`, `sourceforge_user`). Fix: `process()` now tracks whether ANY leg's HTTP response itself succeeded (`any_leg_ok`), separate from whether the parsed body yielded a match; a new pure `all_legs_unreachable(any_leg_http_ok, found_any_entity)` decides the return path — only when every leg failed at the HTTP-status level AND nothing was found does `process()` now return a real `Error::module` (a genuine `ModuleError` event, feeding the existing T2.7 `consecutive_failures` health-signal streak), instead of the prior silent empty success; a leg that responds but simply has no match for this name is unaffected. No replacement endpoint was identified for any of the three legs this cycle (a client-rendered SPA plus two separate WFS/CMS migrations each need their own investigation) — named explicitly as the remaining work, not silently dropped. Module doc comment corrected from the stale "all free, keyless" framing to the confirmed live status. | This project's evidentiary doctrine treats a silently-dead source as worse than a disclosed one: every `FullName` scan run before this fix paid the module's full ~18&nbsp;s timeout budget for three doomed requests and then reported (via the correlator, dossier, and `hse doctor`) that the target simply "has no property record" — a false negative dressed up as a clean result, exactly the "looks like data but isn't" failure mode this codebase's own T2.48–T2.51 provider-integration overhaul was built to eliminate. Surfacing the failure as a real error also feeds the T2.7 scraper-health `consecutive_failures` streak this same node built, so `hse doctor`/the SPA health panel will now correctly flag this source as drifted once real scan history accumulates, instead of reading as a perpetually-quiet, healthy-looking zero. | 4621 lib tests (+3): `all_legs_unreachable_true_when_every_leg_failed_and_nothing_found` and its two false-case siblings pin the exact decision table the fix introduces on the pure, network-free helper `process()` now calls; git-stash-proof since the pre-fix code has no such function (reverting is a compile error, not a passing-then-failing assertion). Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |
| 2026-07-14 | **`extract_anchor_text` stopped at the FIRST `href="…"` occurrence in a result's own SERP markup, so a title-bearing anchor repeated later in the same card was never reached — closing the title-extraction defect the T2.7 sixth (Startpage) slice explicitly named as its next candidate rather than rush into that commit. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.95 / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-HEALTH-SIGNAL (extended).** The real Startpage capture (`fetch/testdata/startpage_kylo4kylo.html`) repeats each genuine result's own URL 4 times per card, in a consistent document order: a textless "favicon-link" icon wrapper first, a short site-name anchor, a display-URL anchor, then the actual titled `<h2>`-wrapped link last. `extract_anchor_text`'s old `html.find(&search_dq)` matched only the first (textless) occurrence, returned empty, and the caller fell back to `extract_surrounding_text`'s fixed ±300-char window — which for this exact markup shape grabbed whatever visible text happened to sit nearby instead of the real title. Confirmed the concrete failure against the unfixed code (`git stash` on the one changed file, real capture re-run): the Instagram result's title came out empty, and the other 3 genuine results' titles came out as "Visit in Anonymous View" — Startpage's own adjacent proxy-link label, real visible text belonging to the PRECEDING result card, not the one being titled. Fix: `extract_anchor_text` now walks every occurrence of the href in the document and keeps the LAST one whose extracted text is non-empty, rather than stopping at the first match — the observed document order across engines is chrome-first, full-title-last, so a later non-empty occurrence is always at least as trustworthy as an earlier one, and the overwhelmingly common single-occurrence case (href appears exactly once) is unaffected, since the loop still finds and returns that one match. [false-title-fix, evidentiary-integrity, real-data-validated, generalised-fix, golden-fixture-corpus] | A wrong title is an evidentiary defect in its own right, independent of the URL/snippet being correct — an operator or downstream correlation reading "Visit in Anonymous View" as a result's title for a real Instagram/YouTube/NexusMods/UofT-repository hit gets a silently misattributed, unusable label, exactly the kind of "looks like data but isn't" corruption this project's evidentiary doctrine treats as seriously as a false-positive URL. The underlying `extract_anchor_text` primitive is shared by all 17 registered `search_engines` engines, so the fix (walk-and-keep-last rather than stop-at-first) protects every current and future engine whose markup repeats an href for an icon-then-title pair, not just Startpage — the exact generalisation this project's health-signal doctrine (T2.7) has repeatedly favoured over narrow, single-engine patches. Honouring the prior cycle's own explicit scope discipline (naming this as a deferred "next candidate" instead of rushing it into an already-complete slice) and then actually closing it the following cycle is the loop's own idempotency contract working as designed. | 4634 lib tests (+2): a synthetic `extract_anchor_text_skips_textless_occurrences_to_find_the_real_title` pins the general mechanism (icon → site-name → display-url → real title, 4 repeated occurrences, entirely hand-crafted); `parse_results_recovers_real_titles_not_the_preceding_cards_anonymous_view_label` proves the fix against the actual captured bytes, asserting all 4 genuine Startpage titles are recovered exactly and none contain "Anonymous View". Git-stash-proven: reverting `extract_anchor_text`'s multi-occurrence walk alone (`helpers/text.rs` only) reproduces the exact original failure (empty title for Instagram, "Visit in Anonymous View" for the other 3 real results); restored, both new tests pass. All 304 pre-existing `search_engines`-scoped tests continue to pass unchanged (net +2 for this fix). Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |

(2) The Audit tab's footer claimed "Same audit as `hse audit --scan-id <id>`," but `scan_audit` (`src/api/scan_handlers/diagnostics.rs`) unconditionally folds in `fold_events`-derived stored-event signals (module_errors/expansion_stops/excluded_reasons, read straight from the scan's own DB events — no `--log` file needed) and the live cached engine-health sweep, whereas `cmd_audit` (`src/cli/audit/mod.rs`) given only `--scan-id` leaves signals at `LogSignals::default()` — zero source-health signals — unless the operator ALSO passes `--log <debug-file>` (never mentioned in the footer text at all). Fixed both by correcting the copy to state the true relationship between the two interfaces, NOT by changing any computation — the web path's extra completeness in both cases is a feature, not something to remove to match a lesser CLI invocation. No Rust code changed; both fixes are JS-only. [evidentiary-honesty, honest-disclosure, real-data-validated, scope-discipline] | This project's entire premise is evidentiary integrity — a tool whose own UI overstates how another interface would reproduce its results undermines exactly the trust an OSINT/GEOINT platform depends on, especially for the Audit tab, whose whole purpose is self-reporting quality honestly. Root cause traced to the byte level: Startpage's markup repeats the same `href` multiple times per result card (an icon-only "favicon-link" wrapper first, with no text, then the real titled link much later in the same card), and `extract_anchor_text` matches only the FIRST occurrence of a given `href` via a plain `html.find` — the textless icon wrapper — so it falls through to `extract_surrounding_text`'s fixed ±300-char window, which for this specific markup shape reaches backward into the tail of the PRECEDING card (its own "Anonymous View" label sits within 300 bytes behind) rather than forward into THIS card's own real title (positioned much further than 300 bytes ahead, past the icon markup). This is a real, likely-general defect — ANY engine whose markup repeats an `href` for an icon-then-title pair, a common pattern in modern React/SPA-rendered SERPs, could hit the identical failure, not just Startpage — named here explicitly as the next concrete candidate rather than folded into this commit or silently left undocumented. |
| 2026-07-13 | **`extract_surrounding_text`'s title-extraction fallback could leak a preceding `<svg>` icon's raw path data into a result's title as if it were visible text — found investigating the same real Swisscows capture as the sibling false-positive fix, but a genuinely separate title-QUALITY defect. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.88 / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-HEALTH-SIGNAL (new sub-node).** An icon-only social link (`<a href="…" title="Facebook"><svg>…</svg></a>`, no visible anchor text) falls through `extract_anchor_text` to `extract_surrounding_text`'s fixed ±300-char window fallback. This real page packs its social icons back-to-back, so the window's LEFT edge landed strictly INSIDE the immediately preceding icon's own `<svg><path d="M20.376 0H3.624…">` block — that block's opening `<svg>` tag sits further back, outside the window, so `strip_inline_blocks` (which only recognises a *complete* `<tag>…</tag>` pair fully contained in the slice it's handed) never sees it as a block at all, and the raw path-coordinate/attribute text between the window's start and the next tag it DOES recognise (`</path>`, `</svg>`, `<p class="copyright">`, …) reaches the tag-stripping scanner as ordinary visible text. This is a real, previously-undiscovered defect distinct in kind from the URL-level false positive the sibling fix closes in the same investigation — a title-QUALITY bug, not an entity-existence one — and it could affect ANY of the 17 `search_engines` engines wherever an icon-only anchor sits near inline SVG markup, not just Swisscows. Fix: a new `skip_straddling_inline_block` primitive — a single bounded (4,096-byte lookback) forward scan of the region before the naive window start, alternating between "outside a block" (locate the next `<svg`/`<style`/`<script`, whichever comes first in document order) and "inside a block" (locate that specific tag's own close) — correctly threads through any sequence of complete, back-to-back blocks (exactly the shape the real capture has: one icon's `</svg>` immediately followed by the next icon's `<svg>`) rather than a naive per-tag-type "find the last occurrence" scan, which could wrongly pick an already-closed block over a still-open earlier one when multiple tag types are nearby. When the naive window start lands strictly inside an unclosed block, it now snaps forward to just past that block's close tag before the window is ever sliced out and handed to `strip_tags`. [refactor-shaped-repair, evidentiary-integrity, real-data-validated, generalised-fix, scope-precision] | A garbled title (raw SVG path coordinates and XML attribute syntax standing in for a human-readable result title) is a real evidentiary-quality defect on its own — even setting aside the specific URLs the sibling fix already excludes, the underlying `extract_surrounding_text` primitive is shared by every one of the 17 registered search engines, so this same failure mode was silently available to corrupt a title on ANY engine's genuine (non-excluded) result whenever its markup happened to place SVG icons near an untexted anchor. Fixing the shared primitive protects every current and future engine at once, rather than only patching the one instance this investigation happened to surface. | 4619 lib tests (+2): `extract_surrounding_text_does_not_leak_a_straddling_svg_paths_raw_data` uses a deliberately long (400-byte) synthetic SVG path — long enough that its opening tag sits more than 300 bytes before the anchor while its closing tag sits within the last 300, genuinely reproducing the straddle rather than a short fragment a ±300 window would swallow whole — and asserts both that the raw path/attribute text is excluded AND that genuine nearby visible text ("Real Co") is still correctly kept (guarding against an overzealous fix that also drops legitimate content). `extract_surrounding_text_excludes_swisscows_own_icon_svg_path_from_a_real_capture` proves the same fix against the actual real captured bytes, not only a hand-crafted case. Git-stash-proven: reverting the `skip_straddling_inline_block` call site in `extract_surrounding_text` makes both new tests fail (the raw SVG path/`clip-rule` text reappears); restored, both pass. All 301 pre-existing `search_engines`-scoped tests continue to pass unchanged. LIVE-VERIFIED (honest disclosure): a real `hse scan --kind name --value Kylo4kylo --modules search_engines` run completes cleanly with zero errors, but this specific canonical seed's own triggering URLs are the same ones the sibling fifth-slice fix already excludes BEFORE title extraction ever runs, so this exact live scan cannot newly demonstrate a would-have-been-garbled title turning clean — the real-capture-backed unit test above is offered as the direct, honest evidence instead of a fabricated live claim. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |

The REAL, verified blocker: `base_url()` resolves to `see-know.icu`, which IS reachable from this sandbox (unlike `see-know.eu`, which the embedded key's own doc comment previously — incorrectly — blamed for "a live probe could not be run here"). A real `hse scan --kind domain --value wikipedia.org --modules see_know` run against the reachable `.icu` domain logged, via the actual production client code path: "SeekNow (see-know.eu) lookups disabled: the API key was rejected (invalid_api_key)" — the embedded `SEEKNOW_DEFAULT_KEY` is confirmed DEAD, not merely untested. This failure is completely silent to an operator: the scan reports `see_know: found 0`, byte-for-byte indistinguishable from a legitimate empty result. (2) Added a new "SeekNow account" section to `hse doctor`, mirroring the existing WiGLE account-health block exactly: probes `/credits`, then reports `INVALID — the configured key was rejected. Set a valid, plan-enabled key via HUNTSMAN_SEEKNOW_KEY or the UI Settings panel.` when `is_key_invalid()` comes back true, or the remaining/daily-limit credits otherwise. (3) Corrected the `SEEKNOW_DEFAULT_KEY` doc comment, which previously blamed the WRONG domain (`.eu`) for an untested status, to record the actual confirmed-dead finding against the real `.icu` domain the code uses — an honest starting point for whichever future cycle rotates in a working key. A diagnostic tool (`hse doctor`) whose own code comments claim a check exists that was never actually wired is itself a form of silent breakage — worse than no comment at all, since it actively misleads a future maintainer into believing the gap is already closed. Declining to invent a new "keyword-based credential search" endpoint (the other plausible reading of "proactively discover API keys") instead of fabricating an unverified capability matches this project's own prior-established discipline (the exact same class of speculative scaffolding T2.58/T2.62 already found and removed once) — investigating and finding the REAL blocker (a confirmed-dead key with no diagnostic) was more valuable than guessing at a new feature the API may not even support. | 4606 lib tests (+5): new tests pin every `parse_credits_body` classification path — an `invalid_api_key` body and a `plan_required` body both classify `AuthError`; all 4 documented credits-response shapes (`credits_remaining`+`daily_limit`, nested under `data`, `remaining`/`total`, nested under `credits`) correctly extract `(4200, Some(5000))`; a shape missing `daily_limit` still extracts `remaining` with `None`; and 4 garbage/non-JSON bodies (empty, plain text, an HTML error page, and `{}`) all correctly classify `Unparseable`, NEVER `AuthError` — the critical distinction that keeps a transient network blip from ever being mistaken for a confirmed dead key and wrongly latching `mark_key_invalid`. Git-stash-proven: neutering the `is_auth_error` check inside `parse_credits_body` fails 2 of the 5 new tests (the two auth-rejection cases) exactly as the classification bug predicts; restored, all pass. | 2026-07-13 | **Merged `username_search`'s, `social_probe`'s, and `streaming_probe`'s identical `detection_strength` presence-confidence tiering into a new `util::probe_confidence`. Each module's own `detection_strength` kept its existing per-module signature — a single shared signature across all three isn't possible, since `&Platform` and the two distinct `&Detect` enums are structurally different types — but now computes its own single boolean ("did this rule inspect the body?") and delegates to the shared function, reducing each to a one-line wrapper. Consolidating to one source makes that class of silent three-way drift structurally impossible going forward. | 4599 lib tests (+2): two new `util::probe_confidence` unit tests pin the exact tuple values. Regression-proven by corrupting the shared function (swapping which branch returns which tuple) — 7 tests failed simultaneously (the 2 new unit tests plus 5 pre-existing tests spanning `social_probe`, `streaming_probe`, and `username_search`), proving the three modules are now genuinely single-sourced and can no longer silently drift apart from each other; restored, all pass. All pre-existing tests in the three affected modules continue to pass unchanged (pure delegation, zero behavior change). LIVE-VERIFIED: a real `hse scan --kind username --value octocat --modules username_search,social_probe,streaming_probe` run completes cleanly with both the body-verified (0.92) and status-only (0.74) confidence tiers correctly represented among the entities and correlator output, zero errors across all three merged call paths. | 2026-07-13 | **Merged `web_crawler::extract_api_keys_from_body` and `username_search::scan_text_for_keys`'s duplicate, inferior key-tokenizer loops onto the shared `util::found_keys::key_tokens`, fixing a real long-credential truncation bug. Concludes the operator's "REFACTOR and merge where it is advantageous" request. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.80 / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-KEYHARVEST-RELOCATE (extended).** Both functions hand-rolled the identical `body.split(whitespace/quote-chars)` + hardcoded `16..=200`-char-window loop instead of reusing `found_keys::key_tokens` — the canonical tokenizer `util::extract`/`util::http::keys` already use for the same job. Reading `found_keys::key_tokens`'s own constants confirmed two real defects the duplication had accumulated: (1) its 200-char cap is stricter than `found_keys::MAX_TOKEN` (4096), so a real API key/PAT/JWT longer than 200 chars scraped from a crawled page or a username-search profile page was silently dropped, never even reaching classification; (2) its delimiter set (whitespace + `"`/`'`/`` ` `` only) is narrower than the canonical tokenizer's (which also splits on `>`, `<`, `=`, `;`, `,`, `&`, `?`, `{`, `}`, `[`, `]`), so a key embedded as `token=VALUE` or inside a JSON/HTML attribute stayed glued to neighbouring text as one over-long token instead of splitting cleanly into a classifiable candidate. Fix: deleted both ad hoc `body.split(...)` loops; both functions now iterate `found_keys::key_tokens(body, found_keys::MAX_TOKEN)` directly, keeping only their own per-caller metadata (domain-scoped notes/logging for `web_crawler`, the plain "Profile page body" note for `username_search`) — the classification and pooling logic below the loop is unchanged. [architecture-hygiene, reuse-not-duplicate, real-data-validated, silent-breakage-closed] | Two hand-rolled copies of the same tokenizer, each subtly worse than the canonical one, is exactly the drift CONVENTIONS.md's single-sourced-vocabulary rule exists to prevent — and here it had already cost real coverage: any credential longer than 200 characters (a common length for modern PATs, JWTs, and several vendors' rotated API keys) scraped from a crawled web page or a username-search profile page was silently invisible to the harvester, with no error or log to reveal the gap. Reusing the canonical tokenizer doesn't just deduplicate code, it recovers that lost coverage and closes the narrower-delimiter gap at the same time, for both call sites at once. | 4597 lib tests (+1): `extract_api_keys_from_body_does_not_truncate_at_the_old_200_char_cap` constructs a 234-char BinaryEdge-shaped (`bp0_`-prefixed, poolable) key — longer than the old 200-char cap but under the real `found_keys::MAX_TOKEN` (4096) — and asserts it now reaches `pool.add` — git-stash-proven by reverting the tokenizer call back to the old inline `body.split(...)` + `16..=200` window, which fails the assertion exactly as the truncation bug predicts; restored, it passes. The pre-existing `extract_api_keys_from_body_gates_length_and_rejects_non_poolable` characterization test continues to pass unchanged (its 3 cases are unaffected by the tokenizer swap: the length-gated short tokens, the non-poolable github token, and the placeholder rejection all resolve identically under the richer delimiter set). Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |

Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.79 / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-KEYHARVEST-RELOCATE (extended).** `api_key_probe/probes.rs` maintained its own independent url/header/env_var table alongside `util::service_defs`, and the two had already drifted apart on 3 real vendor endpoints — `securitytrails` tested `/v1/account/usage` in one table and the documented key-test endpoint `/v1/ping` in the other; `virustotal` tested a URL-analysis endpoint that can't validate a bare key in one vs a real identity-check endpoint (`/v3/users/me`) in the other; `greynoise` tested the free/unauthenticated community endpoint in one — flagged by `api_key_probe`'s OWN code comment as a false-positive risk — vs the real paid endpoint in the other. A 4th drift: `api_key_probe`'s censys entry referenced `HUNTSMAN_CENSYS_KEY`, an environment variable that exists NOWHERE else in the codebase (the real `censys` module and `.env.example` both use the paired `HUNTSMAN_CENSYS_ID`/`HUNTSMAN_CENSYS_SECRET`, which `service_defs` already had correct). Fix: extended `ServiceDef` with an optional `probe_parser` field — the one genuinely per-vendor part of the old duplicate table (each service's distinct JSON response shape) — and derived the request (URL + auth header) generically from the already-declared `test_url`+`key_header` via a new `request_for()`, since every `KeyPlacement` variant maps onto exactly one request shape. Deleted `api_key_probe`'s entire parallel table rather than reconciling it by hand a second time (the drift's actual root cause). Fixed the 3 drifted URLs to their more-correct (previously `api_key_probe`-side) values, now single-sourced so they structurally cannot re-drift. WiGLE's (and censys's) real limitation — both actually require HTTP Basic Auth over a username:token PAIR, which neither table's single-value `KeyPlacement` can express — was explicitly documented as a genuine, pre-existing gap in BOTH source tables (not introduced by this merge, not silently papered over), correctly deferred as its own future fix needing a paired-credential `KeyPlacement` variant. [architecture-hygiene, single-sourced-vocabulary, real-data-validated, honest-disclosure, reuse-not-duplicate] | Two independently-maintained tables serving the identical purpose (key-endpoint validation) is exactly the drift CONVENTIONS.md's single-sourced-vocabulary rule exists to prevent — and it had ALREADY produced 4 real, silently-wrong facts before anyone noticed, three of which meant a real, valid key for that service would be tested against the wrong endpoint by one of the two validators and could be mis-reported invalid. Consolidating to one source doesn't just tidy the code — it makes such drift structurally impossible going forward, the actual point of the refactor. Correctly declining to also "fix" WiGLE's Basic-Auth encoding as a drive-by matters because a real fix needs a paired-credential concept neither table has today; silently picking one of the two already-wrong behaviors and calling it fixed would have been a fabricated correctness claim. | 4596 lib tests (+1): `probe_request_uses_the_single_sourced_service_def_not_a_duplicate_table` asserts the 3 corrected URLs (securitytrails, virustotal, greynoise) — git-stash-proven by reverting greynoise's `test_url` to the old wrong community endpoint, which fails the assertion exactly as the drift bug predicts; restored, it passes. All pre-existing `api_key_probe`/`service_defs`/`key_pool` tests (52 total across the three modules) continue to pass unchanged after the merge. LIVE-VERIFIED: a real `hse scan --kind apikey --modules api_key_probe` run against this operator's own REAL, currently-configured HIBP key was executed TWICE — once against the merged code, once against the original pre-merge code (via `git stash`) — and produced byte-for-byte IDENTICAL 20-entity output both times, proving the merge introduced zero behavioral regression. That same live comparison also surfaced a separate, genuinely PRE-EXISTING false-positive pattern (several services unrelated to HIBP also reporting "validated") — confirmed unchanged by the identical before/after live comparison, and correctly logged as its own distinct future finding (a deeper `is_error_response`/`parse_info` gap: a probe is marked "validated" once its response merely fails to match known error shapes, without requiring `parse_info` to have extracted any real account data) rather than fabricated as part of this merge's scope. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |

| 2026-07-13 | **Fixed `au_people`'s dead White Pages AU dispatch leg — the same root-cause class as the AEC finding below: a legacy server-rendered search URL retired in favour of a client-rendered SPA, silently paying a wasted request/timeout cost on every scan. Removed the dead White Pages AU dispatch from `process()`. Rather than keep the now-unreachable `parse_whitepages_html` parser (and its `clean_au_locality` helper) dormant "for a future repoint," deleted both outright, consistent with this session's repeated dead-code doctrine (T2.52 `mls`, T2.58–63): a future repoint would need an entirely new parser for whatever data shape the real underlying API returns (likely JSON, given the Nuxt SPA architecture), not a rehabilitated HTML parser built for the retired server-rendered shape. Also fixed a real correctness bug this leg's removal exposed: the module's Person-anchor evidence hardcoded `"source": "whitepages_au+tps_au"` regardless of which leg actually contributed — now that White Pages can NEVER contribute, that claim was wrong 100% of the time, corrected to `"tps_au"`. True People Search AU was deliberately NOT touched — unreachable from this sandbox (502 from the outbound proxy), so its live status is honestly undetermined this cycle. | 2026-07-13 | **New correlator rule AU-112 closes the `Cidr` correlator gap named in a prior cycle's rule-gap search, reusing an existing primitive instead of re-implementing it. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` C1 (last named thread) / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-CORR.** A prior rule-gap search flagged `Cidr` as a plausible correlator gap ("shared-CIDR-block hosting could indicate common infrastructure") but assessed it as blocked on "real CIDR-containment computation... a new capability." That primitive already existed and was simply unused outside its own module: `util::spf::Ipv4Cidr`/`Ipv6Cidr` (`.parse`/`.contains`), overflow-safe, already tested, built originally for SPF `ip4:`/`ip6:` mechanism parsing (RFC 7208). New `rule_au_112_shared_cidr_infrastructure`: an independently-discovered `IpAddress` entity (e.g. from DNS resolution) falling inside a `Cidr` entity (e.g. from an ASN/BGP lookup) discovered in the same scan is emitted as a shared-hosting-infrastructure signal — Medium severity, explicitly framed as shared infra/hosting rather than personal ownership, matching AU-111's precedent for inferred infrastructure signals. Scoped conservatively per this project's accuracy-over-coverage doctrine: a narrow-block floor (`/22` IPv4 — the same "largest block a single operator typically owns end-to-end" floor `netblock`'s own `MAX_HOSTS` comment already establishes; `/48` IPv6 — RFC 6177's smallest end-site allocation) excludes broad ISP/cloud-scale allocations that would otherwise manufacture noise across thousands of unrelated customers, and any IP/block pair `netblock`'s own host-expansion already links explicitly (via a `cidr` evidence attribute) is skipped so the rule adds a genuinely new inference rather than restating an existing one. Added the two missing `prefix_len()` accessors to `util::spf`'s CIDR types (the only actual gap in the primitive) and one new allow-list entry to the `core_does_not_import_util_directly` architecture guard for `util::spf::` — the same pure/dependency-free/no-I/O justification already granted to `util::geohash`/`util::geometry`, not a weakened guard. [correlation-depth, capability-gap-closed, reuse-not-duplicate, real-data-validated, accuracy-over-coverage] | Cross-referencing every `EntityKind` against the correlator's actual rule coverage previously found `Cidr` a legitimate gap but incorrectly assessed it as needing new capability work; re-discovering that the needed primitive already existed elsewhere in the codebase (built for an unrelated purpose) and reusing it exactly matches this project's Rule 4 doctrine (one classifier/finder, never a duplicate) and turned an assessed multi-cycle capability build into a single focused rule addition. The narrow-block floor is the same "accuracy over coverage" discipline behind `sanctions_ofac`'s deliberately-lowered `HIT_CONFIDENCE` and `bsb.rs`'s stable-allocations-only table: a rule that fires on every /8 an ISP owns would bury real shared-infrastructure findings in noise from unrelated customers of the same provider. | 4599 lib tests (+6): `au112_fires_when_an_independently_discovered_ip_falls_in_a_narrow_block`, `au112_does_not_fire_for_an_ip_outside_the_block`, `au112_does_not_fire_for_a_broad_isp_scale_block` (the exact false-positive scenario the narrow-block floor exists to prevent), `au112_does_not_fire_when_already_explicitly_linked` (no duplicate inference over `netblock`'s own explicit host-expansion), `au112_fires_for_a_narrow_ipv6_block`, `au112_does_not_fire_for_a_broad_ipv6_allocation` — 2 confirmed via a neutered-rebuild (function body short-circuited to return empty) git-stash-style proof to fail against the unfixed code and pass after restoring the fix. LIVE-VERIFIED against REAL infrastructure (public organisational infra, not a private individual): a scan of `github.com` through `dns_intel`/`ripestat` resolved real DNS A records and real RIPEstat network-info CIDR blocks; AU-112 correctly fired on genuine narrow-block containments this scan actually produced (e.g. IP `140.82.112.3` inside real block `140.82.112.0/24`) and correctly stayed silent on the same scan's genuinely broader `/17`/`/18` RIPEstat blocks. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures; hand-maintained correlator rule counts (README, `docs/ARCHITECTURE_AUDIT.md`) updated 109→110 total, 97→98 entity-only. |
| 2026-07-13 | **Fixed WiGLE's `verified: Some(false)` account-status latch having no way back to `true` — a sibling stale-attribution bug found continuing the PRIORITY-2 cache/TTL/retry-backoff sweep. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.77 / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-AUDIT-TEMPORAL-SCOPE (WiGLE account-status leg).** `fetch.rs::classify_and_decode` learns WiGLE account verification status as a side effect of ordinary traffic — an HTTP 412 (`"Email is not verified for account"`) calls `account::mark_unverified`, latching `ACCOUNT_STATUS_CACHE.verified = Some(false)` — but the success branch (any 200) did nothing symmetric. In a long-lived `hse serve`/`hse live` process, one 412 anywhere latched "unverified" (surfaced via `hse doctor` and `GET /api/v1/stats`) for the rest of the process, even after the operator completed WiGLE's email-verify step and every later query started succeeding. Fix: new `account::mark_verified(now)`, mirroring `mark_unverified` exactly, called from `classify_and_decode`'s existing success branch — the SAME reactive "learned from live traffic" channel that sets the stale flag now also clears it. No new persistence, no TTL, no invented threshold. [evidentiary-integrity, stale-data-bug, data-freshness, real-data-validated, honest-disclosure] | A diagnostic that keeps warning an operator about a problem they already fixed erodes trust in every OTHER signal the tool surfaces — the same evidentiary-integrity principle behind T2.76's audit-scoping fix, just in the account-diagnostics layer instead of the audit-report layer. The fix costs nothing (reuses the exact mechanism already proven correct for the opposite direction) and closes a real gap: `is_unverified()` currently has no production caller besides diagnostics display, but it's explicitly banked as a future WIRE-IN candidate (a live-dispatch gate) — which would inherit this exact staleness the moment it's wired, so fixing it now is cheaper than fixing it after that wire-in ships. | 4577 lib tests (+1): `mark_verified_clears_a_stale_unverified_latch` — latches via `mark_unverified`, confirms `is_unverified()`, then calls `mark_verified` and confirms it clears the latch and `last_polled_ts` advances to the newer timestamp — git-stash-proven by neutering `mark_verified` to a no-op, which fails the test; restored, it passes. LIVE-VERIFIED against a REAL WiGLE account and a real public-landmark query (Sydney Opera House coordinates, not a private individual's location) via `hse serve`'s HTTP API: `GET /api/v1/stats` went from `{"verified":null}` (never polled) to `{"verified":false,"last_polled_ts":<real ts>}` after a genuine HTTP 412 from this sandbox's actually-unverified configured account — confirming the existing `mark_unverified` half of the mechanism fires correctly end-to-end against real traffic. Honestly disclosed: because the real account here is persistently unverified, a real 200-success response (needed to observe the NEW `mark_verified` half firing from live traffic) was not reachable in this sandbox — that half relies on the git-stash-proven unit test, not a live trigger, exactly as disclosed rather than fabricated. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |
| 2026-07-13 | **Fixed a stale-attribution bug in `hse audit`'s search-coverage finding — the process-global, continuously-refreshed engine-liveness cache was blended into ANY scan's historical report with zero comparison to when that scan actually ran. Found by a cross-cutting PRIORITY-2 sweep of every cache/TTL/quota-budget/retry-backoff mechanism in the engine. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.76 / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-AUDIT-TEMPORAL-SCOPE.** `engine_health_signals()` in `api/scan_handlers/diagnostics.rs` read `search_engines::health::cached()` — a single snapshot of "conditions right now", refreshed every 900s by `hse serve`'s periodic sweep — and stamped it into the audit report for whatever scan id was requested, never reading `HealthSnapshot.checked_at` against the scan's own `finished_at`. Two real failure directions: a scan with full coverage, audited weeks later after 3 engines break upstream, gets a spurious "reduced discovery coverage" finding it never had (false positive); a scan that genuinely ran during a real outage, audited after recovery, gets no coverage-gap finding at all — silently hiding a real historical limitation (false negative). Fix: `scan_audit` now also reads the scan record (free — folded into the same `spawn_blocking` batch as entities/events) and passes its `finished_at.unwrap_or(started_at)` to a new pure `snapshot_still_relevant_to(checked_at, scan_reference_ts)`, which gates the cached snapshot's application to within 2× the health sweep's own declared refresh cadence — reusing a new `health::DEFAULT_REFRESH_SECS` constant (single-sourced with `cli/serve`'s previously-independent hardcoded `900`) rather than inventing a threshold, and adding no new persistence. A snapshot older than the scan (cache hasn't caught up to a just-finished scan yet) is never rejected — that's the pre-existing, separately-handled "no data yet" case (`cached()` returning `None`), not a misattribution. [evidentiary-integrity, stale-data-bug, data-freshness, real-data-validated, no-invented-threshold] | An audit report exists to explain ONE scan's actual results; silently substituting today's unrelated conditions for that scan's own era is exactly the kind of finding that outruns its evidence this project's evidentiary-integrity mission forbids — whether it inflates a clean scan's apparent weaknesses or erases a real one. The fix reuses the sweep's own already-declared cadence as the tolerance rather than picking an arbitrary number, and needed zero new persistence since the scan's own timestamp was already one field away in an existing query. | 4576 lib tests (+4): `a_snapshot_taken_shortly_after_the_scan_is_relevant`, `a_snapshot_from_well_before_the_relevance_window_expires_is_relevant`, `a_snapshot_from_long_after_the_scan_is_not_relevant` (the exact false-positive scenario the finding named), `a_snapshot_older_than_the_scan_is_never_rejected_here` — git-stash-proven by neutering `snapshot_still_relevant_to` to always return `true`, which fails the weeks-later test; restored, all pass. LIVE-VERIFIED against REAL, naturally-occurring conditions (no synthetic fixture, no clock manipulation): a real scan from ~2 hours earlier in this same session (genuinely past the 1,800s tolerance) was audited immediately after a fresh live 17-engine sweep found this sandbox's real, currently-degraded network (11 blocked, 1 down, only 5 up) — pre-fix this would have stamped that old scan's report with the current outage; post-fix, `GET /api/v1/scans/{id}/audit` correctly returned `engines_down: []`, `engines_blocked: []`, `engine_parser_defects: []`. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures. |
| 2026-07-13 | **Built T2.7's parse-rate/zero-yield drift-detection leg — a module that completes without erroring but silently returns zero results, distinguished from a genuinely-empty target. Closes T2.7 (parse-rate leg) / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-HEALTH-SIGNAL.** The hard-failure leg (consecutive `ModuleError`s) already shipped; this is the sibling signal T2.7's original sketch also named. Rather than inventing a statistical yield baseline (average/median historical results, with an arbitrary drop-percentage threshold no real incident has yet justified), this reuses the exact three-strikes shape already validated for hard failures: `SourceHealth::is_yield_drifted()` is true only when a source has BOTH `ever_yielded` (found something at least once anywhere in the queried window — proof it's capable) AND `consecutive_zero_yield >= YIELD_DRIFT_THRESHOLD` (3, same rationale as the existing `DRIFTED_THRESHOLD`). A source that has never yielded is never flagged no matter how many trailing zeros (that's a legitimately-empty target, not drift); a source whose newest run recovers has its streak correctly closed at 0, not inflated by older zeros; `ModuleError` events are skipped entirely for this streak (neither counted nor resetting it), since that failure mode is already `consecutive_failures`'s job. No new persistence — reuses the `found: usize` field `EventKind::ModuleDone` already carries. Wired into `hse doctor`'s "Scraper health" section (new sub-report), `GET /api/v1/health/scrapers` (`yield_drifted`/`yield_drift_threshold`), and the SPA Engines page's Scraper health panel (second table). | 2026-07-12 | **AU-105 under-counted distinct breaches from the SeekNow provider — a drifted breach-name vocabulary silently suppressed credential-reuse findings (3rd slice of the correlator evidentiary-integrity audit). Fix: extended `breach_of` to also read `source_db`. | 2026-07-12 | **Fixed two flaky `util::see_know` cache tests — a real CI-reliability defect in the gate everything else depends on, surfaced during the provider overhaul. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.53 / new `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-CACHE-TEST-ISOLATION.** A full `cargo test` run failed once on `reset_budget_clears_the_cross_module_response_cache` (reproduced at ~1-in-3 full-suite runs). Root cause: `see_know`'s `RESPONSE_CACHE` is a process-global `static` cleared by ANY concurrent scan-running test in the whole binary (via `install_core_hooks`'s `reset_per_scan` → `see_know::reset_budget` → `RESPONSE_CACHE.clear()`); two tests did a read-after-write on it (`cache_put(k)` then asserting `cache_get(k)` present), and an unrelated test's cache-clear landing in that window spuriously failed the sanity assertion. The existing `BUDGET_TEST_LOCK` only serialises tests WITHIN `see_know/tests.rs`, so it cannot cover the external clearers — confirmed empirically with a temporary deterministic stress reproduction (a background thread hammering `reset_budget()` under the lock still failed the read-after-write against out-of-file scan tests). Fixed by making both read-after-writes robust: retry the `cache_put`+`cache_get` up to 200× until the unique key is observed present (an external clear landing every single time is astronomically unlikely), then keep the real contract assertion unchanged (`reset_budget()` must clear the unique key — robust, since nothing else ever puts it). [test-reliability, correctness, tests] | A flaky test in the verification gate directly undermines every other cycle's "full gate green" claim — the entire autonomous loop rests on the gate being trustworthy, so a ~1-in-3 spurious failure is a real correctness defect in the process itself, not cosmetic. Fixing it at the read-after-write (rather than papering over with a retry-the-whole-suite CI hack) keeps the test's real contract intact while removing the shared-global race. | No new product test (a test-hygiene fix); the two existing tests were made robust and the temporary deterministic stress reproduction was removed after confirming the diagnosis (it inherently races the out-of-file clearers a same-file lock can't serialise). Proof is by stability: the full lib suite, previously failing ~1-in-3, now passes **8/8** consecutive runs. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures (4601 lib tests). |
