Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.48 / new `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-PROVIDER-OVERHAUL (`[~]`).** A live probe of the real `api.domainsdb.info/v1/domains/search` (three real brand keyword × zone queries: google/com, microsoft/net, amazon/io) returned a consistent `401 {"error":"API key required","message":"Anonymous access is disabled. Please sign in to obtain an API key…"}`. The proxy was confirmed healthy (no relay failures, domainsdb.info not proxy-blocked), so this is the provider's own policy, not a sandbox artifact. The module was registered `ModuleCost::Free` and documented "free, no key, unlimited," but its per-zone loop did `if !r.status().is_success() { continue; }` — swallowing the 401 silently — so on every Domain/Organisation/FullName scan it made six doomed requests and emitted nothing, with no `ModuleError` and no `ModuleSkipped`: a phantom zero-yield. Fix: reclassified `Free`→`KeyGated` (per-tier counts are computed dynamically from `cost()`, so no guarded count drifts; total module count unchanged), registered `HUNTSMAN_DOMAINSDB_KEY` in `util::keys::KNOWN_KEYS` + a `signup_hint`, resolve the key first via `ctx.key(KEY_ENV)?` (unset → clean "needs key" skip), send `Authorization: Bearer <key>`, and report a `401`/`403` on a configured key to the key pool (`ctx.report_key_exhausted`) + break the zone loop instead of swallowing it. Confirmed the "populate" mandate boundary via `AskUserQuestion`: wire in the existing key machinery, no external account registration or credential harvesting. [correctness, robustness, evidentiary-honesty, code+tests] | A silently-dead source is worse than a disclosed one for an evidentiary OSINT tool: it reads as "no look-alike/typosquat domains exist for this subject" when the truth is "the source was never queried successfully" — precisely the false-negative-dressed-as-a-clean-result failure this project's doctrine treats as worse than missing coverage. This is also the model finding for the whole provider-integration overhaul: a live contract check (not a code read) is the only thing that catches a provider silently changing its access policy out from under a module that still "looks built." | 2 new/changed regression tests in `modules::domainsdb::tests`: `cost_is_keygated` (replacing `cost_is_free`) and `missing_key_yields_a_clean_needs_key_skip_not_a_silent_empty` (drives the real `process()` with an empty key map, asserts `Error::MissingKey(HUNTSMAN_DOMAINSDB_KEY)`), git-stash-proven to fail against the pre-fix module (compile error — the fix's `KEY_ENV`/`KeyGated` don't exist) and pass against the fix. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures (4608 lib tests, +1). Live-verified against the REAL provider with the real binary: (1) no key → `dispatch` then `skipped — needs key HUNTSMAN_DOMAINSDB_KEY` on a real `github.com` scan (the corrected honest state, vs. the pre-fix silent empty); (2) bogus key → one real Bearer-authenticated dial to `api.domainsdb.info` returning `403 {"Insufficient credits"}`, detected and broken-on after a single zone (not six wasted requests), key reported to the pool. |
| 2026-07-12 | **WiGLE discarded its own server-computed `retry_secs` on a 429 instead of acting on it — the third and final finding from the background data-freshness/pacing audit, re-confirmed this cycle against a fresh real-scan debug bundle the operator supplied. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.47 / new `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-WIGLE-RETRY-AFTER.** `fetch_wigle_typed`/`fetch_wigle_ssid` computed `retry_secs` from a 429 response's real `Retry-After` header purely to log a warning, then discarded the value and returned a hard `Error::module` whose `to_string()` embeds the standalone token `429` — so the shared per-module circuit breaker (correctly, following the T2.45 fix earlier this cycle) hard-trips WiGLE for the fixed 600s `RATE_LIMIT_COOLDOWN` regardless of what the server's own header actually asked for. New `get_with_retry` — a small shared helper replacing the near-duplicated inline 429/412/error handling both search endpoints previously carried verbatim — retries a 429 once, sleeping the server's real `Retry-After` value bounded to a new `RATE_LIMIT_RETRY_CAP_SECS` (4s) so the sleep always fits inside the module's 20s `max_timeout_ms` budget across its four sub-fetches (WiFi bbox, WiFi SSID, cell, Bluetooth). A persistent 429 (the retry also rate-limited) still degrades to `Error::RateLimited` and the same module-error/circuit-breaker path as before — no infinite retrying. The supplied real debug bundle (a live "Riley Morley" scan) itself never exercised WiGLE (its target didn't satisfy WiGLE's `accepts()` gate), so this fix's own live verification used a separate real scan instead. [correctness, throughput, code+tests] | Over-throttling a paid, quota-limited provider past its own real rate-limit contract wastes exactly the collection depth the operator is paying for — the same "how often can this actually be used before backing off" concern the operator's original diagnostic request (which opened this entire finding class, closed across T2.44/T2.45/T2.46/T2.47) asked about. Acting on the server's own `Retry-After` instead of a fixed worst-case default recovers a short burst throttle within the same scan instead of losing the rest of that scan's WiGLE coverage to an oversized cooldown. | 2 new regression tests (`get_with_retry_recovers_from_a_429_using_the_servers_real_retry_after`, `get_with_retry_gives_up_after_one_retry_on_a_persistent_429`) drive a REAL local `tokio::net::TcpListener` server — the same pattern already established in `util::http::tests` for exactly this class of HTTP-status test, no new mock-server dependency — through the real, unmodified `get_with_retry` function over real sockets. Both confirmed via `git stash` to fail as a compile error against the pre-fix code (the function didn't exist) and pass post-fix. Gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures (4607 lib tests, +2). Live-verified: built the real `hse` binary and ran `hse scan --kind coordinates --value -27.4698,153.0251 --depth 1 --output json` (a real Brisbane CBD target) against a fresh throwaway `$HOME`; the log confirms WiGLE made real HTTP round-trips to `api.wigle.net` through the exact fixed code path and completed cleanly (`"done","module":"wigle","found":0`) with zero errors or panics. The live API did not itself return a 429 in this run, so the retry branch wasn't exercised live; deliberately hammering a real paid provider to force a genuine 429 on demand would be abusive to a real account and was not attempted — named honestly as the responsible fallback the local-server test covers instead. |
| 2026-07-12 | **`search_engines::SESSION_EMPTY_COUNTS` never reset per-scan — the second finding from the same background data-freshness/pacing audit, the same bug class as the T2.44 stale-cache fix. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.46 / new `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-SEARCH-LIVENESS-RESET.** `SESSION_EMPTY_COUNTS` is a process-global `Mutex<HashMap<&'static str, EngineLiveness>>` that silences a search engine for the rest of ONE scan once it misses enough consecutive seeds in a row (correct by design), and exempts a "proven live" engine (≥1 real result this session) from the aggressive threshold in favour of a tolerant one — also correct by design, WITHIN one scan. But unlike `oathnet_pro`/`see_know`/`wigle`'s per-scan state, it was never wired into `modules::install_core_hooks`'s `reset_per_scan` hook — confirmed by directly reading the hook body. Under a long-lived `hse serve`/`hse live` process this meant an engine silenced by a genuine block streak against one target stayed silenced for every LATER scan against a completely different target, indefinitely, with no basis for assuming that engine will fail the same way against unrelated targets. The milder half of the same leak: a "proven live" exemption also carries across scan boundaries, costing at most a few extra retries rather than dropped results. New `search_engines::reset_session_liveness()` clears the entire map; called from `reset_per_scan` alongside the existing three providers' resets. [correctness, throughput, code+tests] | This is the exact same failure shape the register logged for the paid-API response cache (`RESPONSE_CACHE`) just above: process-global scan-scoped state that outlives the scan boundary it's meant to respect. Here it costs coverage rather than freshness — a healthy search engine silently stops contributing results to a brand-new scan purely because of what happened in an earlier, unrelated scan in the same long-lived process, exactly the kind of hidden degradation an operator running `hse serve`/`hse live` for extended periods would have no way to notice. | 1 new regression test (`reset_session_liveness_clears_silenced_and_proven_state_across_scans`), confirmed via `git stash` as a compile error pre-fix (the function didn't exist yet, not a silent pass) and a runtime pass post-fix. Gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures (4605 lib tests, +1). Live-verified: a real `hse serve` process ran the real `reset_per_scan` hook — including the new call — cleanly across two distinct real scan IDs (`selftest` at startup, then a real issued API scan against the project's own canonical acceptance-test seed `Kylo4kylo`) with zero panics or errors from the new code path; naturally reproducing the exact silence-then-unsilence symptom across a genuine real block streak would need a longer-running live session than this sandboxed pass covered, noted honestly rather than overclaimed — the guarantee the fix makes (the map is unconditionally cleared) is what the regression test pins regardless of whether that exact scenario was observed live. |
| 2026-07-12 | **Hardened `core::engine::circuit::is_rate_limited` against bare-substring false positives — a regression surfaced by the background data-freshness/pacing audit. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.45 / new `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-CIRCUIT-TOKEN-ANCHOR.** The classifier's vocabulary included the bare single words `"exceeded"`/`"credit"` and unanchored `429`/`402` digit matching — any of which could match text with nothing to do with a rate limit: a tokio transport timeout's own message ("deadline exceeded"), scraped breach content mentioning "credit card", or — concretely, for a tool whose own scans routinely surface phone numbers — an echoed subject phone number merely *containing* the digits 429 or 402 (`+61429551402`). Any single such coincidence hard-tripped a healthy module for the full 600s `RATE_LIMIT_COOLDOWN` via `record_error`, silently dropping every subsequent finding it would otherwise have produced for the rest of the scan. A fix for this exact defect had already been written and tested on an unmerged sibling branch (`claude/huntsman-seeknow-api-config-65ow5q`, commit `a5c5fac3`) but never landed on `main` — confirmed via `git merge-base --is-ancestor` (fails) and `git branch --all --contains` (only the sibling branch) — so the regression was live the entire time. Reimplemented fresh this cycle rather than cherry-picked: a curated `QUOTA_PROSE` list of distinctive multi-word compounds replaces the bare tokens, and `429`/`402` now match only as a standalone token (the message is split on non-alphanumeric bytes and compared whole). Anything not caught still falls through to the existing 3-strike soft-failure path. [correctness, evidentiary-integrity, throughput, code+tests] | A false-positive circuit trip degrades scan coverage exactly like a real rate limit does, but is entirely avoidable — a healthy, quota-unexhausted provider silently stops contributing findings for the rest of a scan purely because its own error text happened to contain an unrelated digit run or phrase. For an OSINT tool whose own modules routinely surface phone numbers and financial record text, this was a live, recurring risk rather than a hypothetical edge case. | 3 new regression tests in `core::engine::circuit::tests`: 2 pure-classifier (`is_rate_limited_does_not_misfire_on_timeouts_or_echoed_identifiers`, `is_rate_limited_still_matches_429_402_as_a_standalone_token`) plus 1 full stateful integration test through the real public API (`record_error_with_an_echoed_identifier_does_not_hard_trip_the_breaker`), all three confirmed via `git stash` to fail against the pre-fix classifier and pass against the fix. Gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures (4604 lib tests, +3). Live-verified: built the real `hse` binary and ran `hse scan --kind username --value Kylo4kylo --depth 1 --output json` (the project's own canonical acceptance-test seed) against a fresh throwaway `$HOME`, confirming the fix sits cleanly in the live dispatch path with real network-call error text flowing through `record_error`; the exact coincidental false-positive substring was not naturally reproduced in this specific live run, noted honestly rather than overclaimed as full live reproduction of the exact trigger condition. |
| 2026-07-12 | **Fixed a real cross-scan stale-data bug and a rate-limit/quota-exhaustion conflation in `util::oathnet`/`util::see_know`, in direct response to an operator diagnostic request ("HSE... utilises old data even when it isn't meant to" / "diagnose the Seek API['s]... frequency... before requiring... exponential back off"). Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.44 / new `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-STALE-CACHE-BACKOFF.** Two independent bugs in the shared paid-API client layer. **(a)** `RESPONSE_CACHE` (both providers) dedups identical queries *within one scan* per its own doc comment, but `reset_budget()` — called once per scan specifically so long-lived `hse serve`/`hse live` processes get fresh state each scan — never cleared it, so a re-scan of the same email/username/phone/domain any time later in the process's lifetime silently returned the FIRST scan's cached breach/stealer records, with zero live re-check. **(b)** A transient rate-limit (SeekNow `{"error":"rate_limit"}`, OathNet HTTP 429 — NOT exhausted credits) was classified identically to true daily-quota exhaustion, permanently latching the shared budget for the rest of the scan with zero backoff — every remaining endpoint call for that provider then short-circuited silently. A `RetryStrategy` construct with sensible parameters already existed in `see_know::orchestration` but was entirely dead code (repo-wide grep found zero call sites across ~1,135 lines of `orchestration.rs`/`monitoring.rs`/`force_multiplier.rs` — left untouched, flagged as a separate future decision, not silently ignored). New generic, pure, fully-tested `util::backoff::BackoffPolicy` (no new `rand` dependency) + `core::error::Error::RateLimited` give both clients' retry loops real exponential backoff, reusing the dead constant's own numbers now that they have a live call site. Also reconciled 3 stale quota-figure doc comments. [correctness, evidentiary-integrity, throughput, code+tests] | Silently serving a first-scan's cached breach data on every later re-scan is exactly the "stale data" failure class this project's own evidentiary doctrine treats as worse than missing coverage — an operator re-scanning a target to check for new leaks would see none, not because there weren't any, but because the tool never asked. And a single burst rate-limit response permanently disabling a paid provider for the rest of the scan (with a genuine daily quota still 90%+ unspent) materially undercuts the collection depth the operator is paying for — exactly the "how often can this actually be used before backing off" question the diagnostic asked. | 11 new regression tests: 2 cache-clear tests (one per provider), each confirmed via `git stash` to fail against the pre-fix `reset_budget()`; 7 pure `util::backoff::BackoffPolicy` tests (doubling/capping/jitter-bounds/no-panic-on-pathological-input); 2 SeekNow rate-limit classification tests, confirmed via `git stash` to fail pre-fix. `oathnet::search()`'s live retry loop itself has no independent unit test (no mockable transport exists for the curl-subprocess client — a pre-existing gap, not introduced here). Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures (4601 lib tests, +11). |

| 2026-07-12 | **The full dossier's embedded raw-archive body is now redacted at render time, closing `PROBLEM_TREE` §7 S4's long-standing residual without touching the on-disk archive file.** Investigating S4's suggested fix ("redact the archived success body") surfaced a real policy conflict: `util::raw_archive`'s own doc comment declares the on-disk `raw/*.json` retention a deliberate operator directive — *"never encrypted, hashed, or redacted"* — since it's the record of data the operator paid for. The genuine residual was one step downstream: `cli::export::renderers::render_full`'s "RAW SOURCE RECORDS" section embeds that archived body verbatim into the dossier, and while the auto-written dossier is 0600, an explicit `hse export -o <path>` is deliberately left to the user's umask (S3's own documented boundary) — so an upstream provider echoing our `api_key=…` back in its response could ride a shared/exported dossier out to a world-readable file. [security, evidentiary-integrity, code+tests] | This closes a real, if narrow, key-exfiltration path: an operator who shares an exported dossier (the documented, expected use of `hse export -o`) could otherwise hand a live third-party API key to whoever they shared it with, without realising the dossier's raw-records section carried it. Resolving the conflict at the render layer rather than the archive layer preserves the operator's own explicit "paid data is never redacted" retention policy while closing the actual leak surface. | 1 new regression test (`raw_response_body_masks_an_echoed_api_key_but_keeps_the_rest`) — structural `api_key=` masking, no environment-variable mutation, so it's deterministic under parallel test execution. Closes the last remaining leg of `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.7 / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-HEALTH-SIGNAL.** The cross-scan failure-streak aggregation (`util::scraper_health::aggregate_source_health` over `Store::recent_module_outcome_events`) already existed for the CLI; the API layer's `AppState` holds only `Arc<dyn StoragePort>`, never the concrete `Store`, so reaching the same signal from the web server required a new `StoragePort::recent_module_outcome_events` trait method (default-empty for test doubles, delegating to `Store`'s existing implementation for the real SQLite backend) rather than a storage-layer rewrite. The new panel sits on the Engines page between the search-engine liveness table and the module capability map — the same page an operator already checks for source health, now covering the HTML-parsing modules (au_people, au_electoral, username_search, search_engines, …) alongside the search engines themselves. | 2026-07-11 | **Correlator rules AU-003/AU-038/AU-045/AU-055 no longer manufacture "confirmed"/"verified"/"CRITICAL" certainty from bare status-only username-existence guesses — found via a real OSINT investigation. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.43 / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-WEAK-DETECTION-DISCOUNT.** A username-alias lookup against a Brisbane/Queensland target surfaced `AU-055 [🔴 CRITICAL] "Subject's own confirmed account(s)/profile(s) located across 64 platform(s)... primary sources the subject controls"` and `AU-003 "corroborated by 6 independent source(s) (C_eff=1.000)"` for a single guessed profile URL — investigated rather than trusted at face value, since every one of those hits was tagged `weak-detection`: `username_search`/`streaming_probe` self-report exactly this (a bare HTTP-status match, which a soft-404/SPA-shell can return for almost any handle — 0.74 PROBABLE, versus 0.92 for a real body-marker confirmation), yet AU-003/AU-038/AU-055 checked only the `social-profile`/`confirmed-profile` tag and never the accompanying `weak-detection` one, so the rules' own "confirmed"/"verified" wording directly contradicted the evidence backing them. Root-caused a second, independent defect compounding the first: `webserver_banner`, given a `Url` target, extracts just the host and HEADs the domain *root* (its own `extract_host_port` already discards the path) but re-emitted the entity via `target.to_entity()`, which reuses the full original path — so identical banner evidence for ANY guessed handle on that host counted as an "independent source" corroborating a specific path that was never actually checked. A third gap: `social_probe` — a THIRD module doing the same status-code-existence check across 30 of its 36 platforms — had no weak/verified distinction at all, so fixing only the correlator side would have left this module's identical hits silently un-discounted. [correctness, evidentiary-integrity, correlator, code+tests] | This is the manufactured-corroboration failure class this register has closed three times before (T2.36/T2.37/T2.40), now surfacing on the correlator's synthesis layer instead of a single extractor: a `CRITICAL` "accounts the subject controls" claim built from unverified guesses is exactly the over-credited evidentiary signal this project's own doctrine names as worse than missing coverage, and it would have led an operator to treat a name/handle collision as a confirmed real footprint. Fixing three independent root causes (a mis-attributed domain-root check, four correlator rules blind to their own modules' weak-detection tag, and a third module that never even applied that tag) rather than patching only the symptom the live scan happened to surface means the same false-certainty shape can't reopen through any of the three paths that produced it. | 8 new regression tests: 2 per correlator rule (AU-003/AU-038/AU-045/AU-055 — one proving the weak-only case no longer fires, one proving a genuinely `verified-detection` hit still does), 2 in `modules::webserver_banner::tests` pinning the `Url`→`Domain` host rebase, and 2 in `modules::social_probe::tests` pinning the new weak/verified split against every registered platform. Every test was confirmed via `git stash` to fail against its pre-fix rule/module and pass against the fix. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures (4581 lib tests, +8). |

| 2026-07-11 | **Investigated an apparent P1 evidentiary-integrity defect from the same real "Riley Morley" scan — a US breach-candidate address geo-corroborated at "~0 km" from the Australian subject — and confirmed it does NOT reproduce against HEAD; strengthened permanent regression coverage instead of a fabricated fix.** The operator's debug bundle showed several `oathnet_pro` US breach-candidate `Address` entities (5-digit ZIPs, e.g. `1218 E Grumling Rd., Hodges, Sc, 29653`) carrying a `geo_corroboration` evidence record — "Same-name breach record ~0 km from the subject's confirmed location... confirm this is the subject's own record, not a namesake" — and a `breach-corroborated` tag lifting them out of quarantine to `VERIFIED` confidence. If live today, this would be a serious defect: an unrelated US namesake's leaked breach record confidently declared to BE the Australian subject purely on a geo-match that should be geometrically impossible (South Carolina is not "0 km" from Queensland). Rather than assume the bundle reflected current behaviour and patch blind, reproduced the exact mechanism directly: `promote_breach_candidate_geo_corroborated` (`core/engine/passes.rs`) resolves an entity's locality via `core::geo_family::au_postcode()`, which accepts either a 4-digit trailing token in the entity's own VALUE or a literal `postcode` evidence-attribute key. Built the exact real entity (Address kind, value verbatim, evidence attributes `postal_code`/`addr_postal`/`addr_city`/`addr_street` — the actual keys the US breach data carries, confirmed by reading the debug bundle's full evidence chain) plus the exact real subject anchor (a QLD 4124 `exact-name-match` Address) and called `au_postcode()`/`distance_to_subject()` directly: both correctly return `None`, not `0` — the 5-digit ZIP fails the trailing-token length check, and neither `postal_code` nor `addr_postal` is the literal key `au_postcode()` looks for. The bundle's own environment header (`hse_version: 1.13.0`, `161 registered` modules — the live tree now has 162) confirms this scan predates the current codebase state; the visible false-positive is most likely already closed by the existing `au_postcode_ignores_a_leading_us_street_number` hardening (itself pinned from an EARLIER real scan, `90b936dc…`, per its own doc comment), or a related fix landed since. A second, related thread from the same bundle: two QLD `family-candidate` unclaimed-money addresses (postcodes 4001, 4207) ALSO carried `exact-name-match` even though neither visible register owner ("ANN SQUARE INVESTMENT PTY LTD", "FLANNAN MORLEY & GERALDINE F MORLEY") is a full-name match for the subject — investigated with the same rigor: reproduced `au_unclaimed::qld_helpers::records_to_entities`'s per-record classification with these exact two real records, and it correctly tags BOTH as `family-candidate` only, never `exact-name-match`, confirming the per-record logic itself is sound. Unlike the first thread, this one could not be fully root-caused: without the raw upstream CKAN API response (only the rendered entity view was available), a genuine third exact-match record at the same postcode — invisible in the rendered evidence list because its (source, summary) pair happens to be indistinguishable from what's shown, or genuinely present but not captured in the bundle — remains a live, unruled-out possibility. Logged honestly as unresolved in `PROBLEM_TREE` §6 rather than guessed at or swept aside. [evidentiary-integrity, investigation, code+tests] | This directly answers the operator's own question ("any near Beaudesert Queensland?") by using it as the entry point into a genuine security-relevant audit of the tool's cross-border geo-corroboration logic — exactly the kind of real-scan-triggered investigation this register exists to run. A confirmed-sound verdict, reached by DIRECTLY REPRODUCING the exact real data rather than trusting either the bundle's rendered output or a plausible-sounding theory, is as valuable as a fix: it converts "this looks alarming" into "this is provably handled correctly today, and here is the test that will catch it if it ever regresses" — which is exactly the standard this project's own doctrine sets for verification ("assume latent defects exist until exhaustive investigation and objective evidence prove otherwise"). Equally important is being honest about the SECOND thread's limits: reporting "investigated, correct in isolation, but not fully explainable without more data" is more valuable and more honest than either fabricating a fix for a mechanism that tested clean, or silently dropping the loose end. | 2 new regression tests, both built directly from the real scan's data: `core::geo_family::tests::real_scan_us_breach_address_reproduction` (the exact US breach address + evidence keys + a real QLD anchor — proves `au_postcode`/`distance_to_subject` return `None`, never a false `0 km`) and `au_unclaimed::tests::qld::per_record_address_tags_are_correct_before_any_merge` (the exact two real QLD 4001 records — proves both tag `family-candidate` only). Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures (4573 lib tests, +2). |
| 2026-07-11 | **`search_engines`'s email/phone snippet extraction no longer attributes a completely unrelated third party's contact details to the subject. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.40 / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-SNIPPET-PII-SUBJECT-GATE.** Triggered by an operator supplying a real scan's CSV export and full debug bundle for target "Riley Morley": the export showed an email `pr@rileyjorja.com` at `confidence=0.70 PROBABLE`, whose sole evidence was a Bing snippet for `instagram.com/rileyj/` — a completely unrelated account (first name "Riley" only; "Morley" appears nowhere in the bio, URL, or page title). Traced the exact raw JSON evidence record in the debug bundle (not just the CSV's summary line) to confirm this was a genuine extraction from real snippet content, not a rendering artefact, before concluding anything — the same discipline this register applies to every finding. Root cause: `search_engines/build.rs`'s email/phone extraction (`extract_emails_from_text`/`extract_phones_from_text` run over each result's `title + snippet`) minted an entity from ANY match, unconditionally, with zero check that the specific search result actually names the subject — while the ADDRESS extractor a few dozen lines below it, in the exact same function, already carried precisely this check (`location_on_subject`: requires the distinctive surname token somewhere in the result's snippet/URL for a multi-word name target), built for an earlier live regression (a "Cindy Haynes" scan that trusted a "Cindy He" UNSW staff page's address, injecting a wrong-state location). The proven fix already existed in the file — it had simply never been extended to the two PII kinds most directly actionable when wrong: an incorrect email or phone number names a real third party's actual contact detail as though it belonged to the subject, which is arguably a more serious misattribution than T2.36/T2.37's wrong CONFIDENCE stamp on the subject's own already-correct identifier. Fixed by reuse, not reinvention: hoisted the exact same relevance-check formula to compute once per result before ANY snippet extraction runs, renamed `location_on_subject` → `result_names_the_subject` (the check was never location-specific — it asks whether THIS result actually names the subject, and gates email/phone/address alike), and removed the now-duplicate definition further down. Verified behaviour-preserving for every existing caller before adding anything new: the full pre-existing 290-test `search_engines` suite — including both prior seed-reaffirmation regression tests from the T2.36 fix — passed completely unmodified. [correctness, evidentiary-integrity, code+tests] | This is the same content-blind-attribution defect class as T2.36/T2.37 (the tool's own extraction path manufactures apparent corroboration for content that never actually concerned the subject), found in a THIRD sibling location within the same file, on a HIGHER-stakes PII kind than either prior instance: T2.36 fixed a wrong confidence value on the subject's OWN address; T2.37 fixed a wrong confidence value on the subject's OWN breach record; this fixes an entity that names a DIFFERENT, real, identifiable person's actual email as though it were the subject's — exactly the "false positives are worse than missing coverage" doctrine this project applies to its own output, now demonstrably enforced across all three snippet-derived PII kinds `search_engines` extracts (email, phone, address), not just the one instance a prior cycle happened to investigate. Confirming the fix against the LIVE evidence (the debug bundle's raw JSON, not just the reader-facing CSV) before writing any code is the same "trace complete execution paths... assume latent defects exist until exhaustive investigation proves otherwise" discipline this loop is meant to apply, applied to an operator-supplied real scan rather than a synthetic one. | 2 new regression tests: `email_and_phone_extraction_requires_the_surname_in_the_result` (the T2.40 regression — reproduces the exact real-scan shape: an off-target Instagram-bio-style snippet mints neither an email nor a phone; a genuine on-target snippet with the surname present still mints both) and `email_extraction_unaffected_for_single_token_targets` (proves a username/email seed's extraction, which was never prone to this first-name-collision shape, is untouched by the new gate). The T2.40 regression test was confirmed to fail against the UNFIXED code by reverting the fix via `git stash` and re-running — it minted the real `pr@rileyjorja.com` false positive verbatim, then passed cleanly once the fix was restored. Live-verified beyond the test suite: `hse selftest` 9/9 clean after the change. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures (4571 lib tests, +2). |
| 2026-07-11 | **`hse doctor` gains a per-source scraper health signal — which sources have been failing across recent scans, not just this one. Elevates `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.7 `[ ]`→`[~]`; delivers the hard-failure leg of `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-HEALTH-SIGNAL.** SOL-HEALTH-SIGNAL's own sketch assumed this needed new tracking infrastructure (a `last_success_at`/`consecutive_failures` column or an in-process counter) and had to wait until SOL-F1's `bstr`/`aho-corasick` parser rewrites landed first ("stable enough to measure"). Investigating the actual data model before building anything showed both assumptions were wrong: the engine already persists a `ModuleDone`/`ModuleError` event for every module dispatch, on every scan, TODAY — a request/parse failure is visible in the event log regardless of whether the parser has been rewritten yet. The real, narrower gap: nothing ever aggregated that signal ACROSS scan boundaries, so an operator had no way to see "this source has failed on its last 5 runs" short of manually diffing per-scan verbose logs one at a time. Built two new pieces rather than one new tracking table: `Store::recent_module_outcome_events` (`storage/mod.rs`) — a bounded (5,000-row), newest-first, all-scan_ids SQL query filtered to just `module_done`/`module_error` at the SQL layer (backed by a new `idx_events_type` index), which is naturally a ROLLING window because `events` is already pruned to 7 days / 100k rows by the existing `prune_events` policy — a source that broke and was never scanned again ages out rather than staying flagged forever, matching the honest "recent window" framing rather than claiming permanent historical accuracy it can't back. Second, a new pure module `util::scraper_health`: `aggregate_source_health` walks the newest-first window once, tracking one running per-module accumulator (consecutive `ModuleError`s since the last `ModuleDone`, plus that success's own timestamp), then returns a lexicographically-name-sorted `Vec` — deterministic by construction, no `HashMap`-iteration-order ever reaching the output, and fully unit-testable without a live `Store`. A source is `is_drifted()` once `consecutive_failures ≥ 3` — three strikes, not one, so an isolated transient network timeout doesn't falsely page the operator the way a 1-strike threshold would. Wired into a new "Scraper health (recent window)" section of `hse doctor`: prints how many sources were tracked over how many recent outcome events, and for every drifted module its current streak, its last-success date (via the existing chrono-free `util::timefmt::ymd_utc`), and its most recent error message. [robustness, observability, code+tests] | An OSINT engine whose scrapers parse churning third-party HTML has a real, structural failure mode this project's own doctrine already names: a source's parser silently breaks (layout change, endpoint retirement, rate-limit change) and the operator has no way to notice short of re-reading verbose per-scan logs by hand, across however many scans it takes to notice a pattern — exactly the "high silent-breakage" risk T2.7 was opened to close. Surfacing cross-scan failure streaks in the one place operators already check for tool health (`hse doctor`) converts an invisible degradation into a visible, dated, actionable signal — the same "show your work" evidentiary standard this project applies to OSINT findings, applied here to the tool's own operational honesty about its sources. Correcting the sketch's own stale premise (no new table needed; no dependency on an unrelated in-progress refactor) before building anything is the same investigate-before-assuming discipline the project's harder correctness fixes already apply. | 9 new tests: 7 pure `scraper_health` aggregation cases (healthy source, trailing-failure counting stops exactly at the last success, below-threshold not flagged, never-succeeded-in-window, multiple modules tracked independently + sorted, non-outcome events ignored, empty input) + 1 storage-level `recent_module_outcome_events_filters_orders_and_bounds_across_scans` test proving the SQL filter/newest-first-order/limit contract against a real SQLite round-trip. The pre-existing `open_produces_exact_schema_and_pragmas` exact-schema-enumeration test was updated for the new index — a real, intentional schema addition, not a stale assertion papered over. Live-verified beyond the test suite: a real `hse doctor` run against the operator's own scan database renders the new section correctly (`0 source(s) tracked... no drifted sources` — this database's honest current state, not a fabricated result). Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures (4569 lib tests, +8). |
| 2026-07-11 | **AU-039 ("cryptocurrency wallet linked to identity") no longer attributes a wallet to an unrelated person by alphabetical accident; it now requires a real co-location tie — a shared corroborating evidence source. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.39 / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-AU039-SHARED-SOURCE.** This resolves the design decision the immediately-prior cycle opened T2.39 for and deliberately declined to make blind. The rule (`correlator/rules/crypto.rs`, `rule_au_039_wallet_identity`) previously anchored EVERY `CryptoAddress` in a scan to the single lexicographically-smallest `Person` (else `Email`) UID across the whole confirmed entity set, with zero check that the wallet and that identity shared any evidence, source, or record — so a scan carrying several people (AU-075 alone routinely mints spouse/next-of-kin/emergency-contact/stealer-log-owner `Person` entities) reported one person's wallet as belonging to whichever name sorted first alphabetically. Its own pre-fix test proved this: two clearly-unrelated people ("Aaron Avery", "Zoe Zimmer") produced the identical `High`-severity attribution, differing only by which name sorts first. The deferred question — what relatedness should gate the anchor, and whether the data model even carries that provenance at this call site — was answered by investigating the entity model: `Entity::corroborating_sources()` already exposes each entity's independent evidence sources (the full evidence set minus the non-corroborating replay/enrichment passes) right where the rule runs. The chosen criterion is a **shared corroborating evidence source**: some single collection module surfaced BOTH the wallet and the identity (a stealer log / breach record that names an owner and their wallet in one pass stamps the same `source` on each entity it mints) — a concrete co-location tie, not the mere co-existence-in-the-scan the old rule treated as attribution. A new `shares_corroborating_source(a, b)` helper (`rules/mod.rs`, deliberately built on `corroborating_sources()` rather than the full evidence set, so a `recall` / `cross_scan_history` / `geo_normalize` / `name_intel` replay or self-derivation can't manufacture a shared-source tie — the same honesty rule `source_families` already enforces for cross-family diversity) gates the anchor. The rule no longer picks one global identity: for each wallet it anchors to the source-tied identities (Person preferred over Email; when several of the preferred kind are genuinely tied, EACH is reported as an independent lead — none is arbitrarily singled out), and emits nothing when no identity shares a source with the wallet. Selection is a pure function of the entity set (source membership + UID order), so the live HashMap-ordered pass and the finalise pass agree — the disjoint-set double-persist the old UID tie-break was originally added to prevent stays fixed. [correctness, evidentiary-integrity, code+tests] | This is the same class of defect as T2.36/T2.37 (content-blind attribution) but one layer up, at the correlation-rule level rather than the entity-confidence level: the tool's own correlator fabricated an apparent `High`-severity attribution ("possible attribution" — the language an analyst acts on) between a wallet and a person who share no evidence whatsoever, chosen purely by UID sort order. On a realistic multi-person scan a wallet belonging to one person could be confidently reported as belonging to an unrelated family member or bystander — exactly the false-positive class this project's doctrine treats as worse than missing coverage. | 2026-07-11 | **A codebase-wide precision sweep of `core/`, `util/`, correlator rules, and lint/panic-risk markers found and fixed one real doc/code contradiction, and found-but-deliberately-deferred one real evidentiary-integrity logic weakness. Result was a near-clean sweep: dozens of formulas and thresholds across both directories (ABN/ACN checksums, `key_pool::health_score` weights, the polygon-centroid shoelace formula, Haversine distance, ~20 correlator rule severity thresholds) were independently verified to match their doc comments exactly; a full-repo grep found zero TODO/FIXME/HACK/`unimplemented!()`/`todo!()` markers; all 10 `#[allow(...)]` clippy suppressions in the codebase were read in their surrounding context and confirmed genuinely justified (each either carries an explicit reasoning comment or is test-only/cfg-guarded); and after excluding every `unwrap()` inside a file literally named `tests.rs` or a `#[cfg(test)]` block, exactly 2 production call sites remained (`dehashed/build.rs:105`, `core/graph/mod.rs:196`) and both were confirmed genuinely safe, each immediately preceded by a structural invariant that guarantees the unwrap cannot panic. One real finding did surface: `correlator/rules/gap.rs`'s `AU063_DETAIL_MIN_CONF` constant's doc comment said a detailed AU-063 finding fires "when at least one endpoint is this confident," while the actual gate computes `priority = min(ea.c_effective(), eb.c_effective())` and filters on that — requiring BOTH endpoints to clear the threshold, the logical opposite of the doc's claim; a second copy of the identical drift (in the reverse direction, "the stronger endpoint's... confidence") sat 175 lines below on the `Candidate` struct's own inline comment, making the file internally self-contradictory rather than merely wrong once. Fixed: both comments rewritten to state the true weaker-endpoint/`min` semantics; zero behaviour change, confirmed via `cargo doc` and by independently re-deriving the pre-fix line numbers from `git show HEAD:...` to prove the citation was grounded in real code, not fabricated. A second, genuinely more serious finding — AU-039 (`correlator/rules/crypto.rs`) attributing a cryptocurrency wallet to an ARBITRARY anchor identity (the lexicographically-smallest-uid `Person`/`Email` across the whole confirmed entity set, with zero check that the wallet and that specific person share any evidence) — was deliberately NOT patched in this cycle: the rule's own existing test already proves two unrelated people produce the identical attribution differing only by alphabetical sort order, a `High`-severity misattribution risk realistic on any multi-person scan (which AU-075 alone routinely produces), but a correct fix requires a real relatedness criterion (shared evidence source? a co-occurrence window? the file's own existing `CORROBORATING_FAMILIES` concept?) that this sweep correctly declined to invent unilaterally rather than relocate the arbitrariness with an ad-hoc patch. Opened as new `PROBLEM_TREE` node T2.39 instead. [precision, doc-accuracy, evidentiary-integrity, code] | For an evidentiary tool, a doc comment describing the OPPOSITE of what the code does is worse than no comment at all — it actively misleads a future maintainer into "fixing" already-correct code, or trusting a false operator-facing claim about what gets surfaced as a detailed finding versus consolidated. Equally important: correctly distinguishing a small, unambiguous, low-risk doc fix (implement immediately) from a real logic weakness whose correct fix depends on a design decision nobody should make unilaterally under sweep pressure (defer, document precisely, let a future cycle decide) is itself part of "never fabricate a finding" — rushing AU-039's fix without a real relatedness criterion would not have removed the arbitrariness, only hidden it behind a different-looking but equally ungrounded heuristic. Reporting a near-clean sweep honestly, rather than straining every category to manufacture a finding, is the same anti-fabrication discipline this project applies to its own improvement process, not just to OSINT output. | Doc-only fix, no new test needed or expected (zero behaviour change — confirmed by `cargo doc` clean and the constant/`.min()`/filter all byte-identical pre/post). | 2026-07-11 | **`search_engines` and `see_know` no longer fabricate apparent independent corroboration for a re-pivoted, non-identity target by stamping a flat high-confidence "parent" entity regardless of relevance. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.36 / SOL-LOCATION-SEED-NO-REAFFIRM and new T2.37 / SOL-SEEKNOW-SUBJECT-GATE.** Root cause of the `corroboration=8` pattern found investigating T2.35: HSE re-queues every discovered entity as a new pivot target during expansion, and `search_engines/build.rs` unconditionally stamped a `confidence=0.82` self-referencing parent entity whenever its search returned ≥1 result — with no check that any result actually concerned that specific target. For a re-pivoted Address/Coordinates value (a coarse place, not an identity) this is nearly tautological: real-estate/aggregator sites index almost every US street address regardless of subject relevance, so the re-query always returned something, and the parent — sharing the seed's UID — merged via `Entity::absorb()`'s GREATEST-confidence/unconditional-corroboration-sum semantics straight into the pre-existing entity. A second mechanism (the `location_on_subject` snippet-address relevance gate) was independently confirmed tautological for the same seed kind by an exact tokenization trace: for an address value, `target_terms()`'s last token is the trailing postcode, which every indexing page reproduces verbatim, so the "relevance" check never actually filtered anything. Run as a multi-phase workflow (parallel investigation → implementation → two independent adversarial verification passes → codebase-wide sweep → triage → fix confirmed findings) given the evidentiary-integrity stakes. Fix: a function-scoped `location_seed` check now gates both mechanisms in `search_engines/build.rs` — the parent-entity construction is SKIPPED entirely for a location seed (not merely demoted, since even a lower-confidence parent sharing the seed's UID would still unconditionally inflate `corroboration` via `absorb()`), and the snippet gate short-circuits to `false`. A dedicated post-fix sweep of all 39 non-test `target.to_entity(` call sites across every other module — each independently re-verified against live code, not taken on a prior pass's citation — found exactly one further real instance: `see_know`'s `/search` path minted a `confidence=0.85` `BREACH`-tagged parent on raw, unfiltered hit count rather than an actual subject match, reintroducing the identical bug this same codebase already fixed once in `oathnet_pro` (gated on `matching.is_empty()`, not raw `total_returned`). Fixed identically: a new `search_subject_present()` reuses the same `TargetMatch` machinery already used for per-record demotion. All other 37 call sites were checked and correctly cleared — no findings fabricated to manufacture false urgency, consistent with this project's own anti-fabrication doctrine applied to its own improvement process. [correctness, evidentiary-integrity, code+tests] | This is the single most severe class of bug caught this arc: an evidentiary tool's own re-pivot/search mechanism manufacturing apparent independent corroboration for essentially any address, inflating confidence/classification (many reaching the tool's own VERIFIED tier) regardless of actual relevance to the subject — a live scan showed ~19 mutually-exclusive addresses spanning many different US states all reaching an identical inflated confidence this way. "Never fabricate a finding — false positives are worse than missing coverage" is this project's own explicit doctrine; a tool that silently manufactures corroboration violates that at the confidence-model level, not just in a UI display. Finding and fixing the sibling `see_know` instance via a deliberate, evidence-gated sweep (rather than stopping once the first instance was fixed) closes the same evidentiary gap project-wide instead of leaving a second, identically-shaped hole undiscovered. | 3 new regression tests: `location_seed_pivot_does_not_reaffirm_the_seed_at_0_82`, `identity_seed_still_gets_flat_parent_reaffirmation` (proves no regression for genuine identity seeds), `search_subject_present_gates_on_a_real_match`. Two independent adversarial verification passes (one re-derived correctness from the code and traced concrete cases by hand; one re-ran every gate command from scratch). Live-verified beyond the test suite: a real `hse scan --kind address` with only `search_engines` enabled shows zero `search-enriched` tags and zero 0.82-confidence entities, where the unfixed code would have shown both. Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures (4560 lib tests, +3). |
| 2026-07-11 | **`source_count()` — the count that actually drives `c_effective()` — is now visible everywhere `corroboration` (a different, raw per-module magnitude) is shown, and the SPA's client-side formula mirror now excludes the same 5 sources the backend does, not 2. Closes `PROBLEM_TREE` T2.35 / `SOLUTION_TREE` SOL-CEFF-TRANSPARENCY; opens T2.36 (deeper root cause, not yet solved).** Triggered by the operator supplying a real scan's CSV export and full debug bundle: entities showing `c_eff` exactly equal to base `confidence` despite `corroboration=2/3/9`, and ~19 mutually-exclusive breach-derived addresses (spanning many US states) all sharing an identical `confidence=0.82 corroboration=8`. Two independent research passes established ground truth before writing any fix. First: `c_effective()` is correct and already tested — it uses `source_count()` (distinct, non-enrichment sources), not `corroboration` (summed unconditionally on every merge, by deliberate design, never deduplicated). The real defect was that no export surface — CSV, the debug bundle, the SPA — ever showed `source_count()`, so a reader had no way to tell whether a `corroboration` number meant anything. Fixed across every surface: `render_full` (debug bundle + full dossier) now prints `source_count`, an explanatory note on divergence, and per-evidence non-corroborating markers; `entities_to_csv` gained `source_count`/`corroborating_sources` columns; three stale `core::entity` doc comments (module-level, struct-level, and the `corroboration` field's own comment, which flatly asserted it WAS the corroborating-source count) were corrected to match the real `max(multiplicative, agreement)` formula. Separately, the SPA's client-side `effC()`/`sourceCount()` mirror — added after an earlier over-crediting bug — used an `ENRICHMENT_SOURCES` JS set of only 2 entries (`geo_normalize`, `recall`) against the backend's real 5 (`is_non_corroborating_source` also excludes `name_intel`, `payid`, `cross_scan_history`), so the live Browse dashboard could render a higher C_eff/tier than the server's own authoritative classification for an entity corroborated only by one of the missing three — reintroducing, client-side only, exactly the over-credit bugs those exclusions exist to close. Fixed to match exactly, with a new Rust test that reads the live backend constants and fails on future drift (mirrors the existing `EVENT_TYPES` guard pattern). Second research pass: traced WHY so many unrelated addresses shared `corroboration=8` — found it is NOT `oathnet_pro` (correctly seeds `corroboration: 1` per entity) but `search_engines`' pivot-expansion path, which unconditionally stamps a content-blind `confidence=0.82` parent entity onto any re-queried target with no relevance check, merging via `Entity::absorb()`'s `max()`/unconditional-sum semantics. Root cause pinpointed precisely (`search_engines/build.rs:50`, `:306-307`); deliberately NOT fixed in this commit — opened as new `PROBLEM_TREE` node T2.36 for a properly scoped follow-up rather than a rushed patch to a P1 evidentiary-integrity issue. [precision, transparency, code+tests] | For an evidentiary tool whose core promise is "show your work," a field literally named `corroboration` that does not correspond to the number driving confidence — visible nowhere, on any surface, including the live dashboard operators actually watch — is a real precision defect even though the underlying math was already correct; a human reading a "VERIFIED, corroboration=8" row has no way to know that number is unrelated to why it's VERIFIED. The SPA drift compounds it in the one place a live analyst is most likely to be looking. Tracing the deeper root cause (rather than stopping once the display symptom was fixed) surfaced a materially more serious issue for a future cycle instead of leaving it undiscovered. | 3 new tests: CSV divergence case (`csv_source_count_and_corroborating_sources_reflect_the_filtered_count_not_the_raw_magnitude`), `render_full` note/marker case (`render_full_explains_the_gap_between_corroboration_and_source_count`), SPA drift guard (`spa_enrichment_sources_matches_backend_is_non_corroborating_source`). Full gate green: fmt/clippy `-D warnings`/rustdoc clean, full suite 0 failures (4557 lib tests). |

| 2026-07-05 | **Correctness — `api::update_handlers`'s two update-finish sites now recover from a poisoned mutex the same way `try_start_update` already does, closing a permanent-wedge risk on the self-update status endpoint; plus a minor efficiency tweak in `hacker_news`. Closes new `PROBLEM_TREE` node T2.33.** Subscribed to PR #215's (this arc's rollup) activity and found two unresolved automated review threads. `copilot-pull-request-reviewer` flagged that `try_start_update` (the check-and-claim gate for `POST /api/v1/update/trigger`) already recovers from a poisoned mutex via `.unwrap_or_else(PoisonError::into_inner)` — a deliberate, documented design choice — but the two sites in the spawned update task that record the OUTCOME (`Ok(()) => phase = Restarting`, `Err(e) => phase = Error(...)`) instead used a bare `if let Ok(mut info) = update_info.lock() { .. }`, which silently no-ops on `Err(PoisonError)`. Independently verified by direct read before acting: confirmed this could strand `phase` at `Applying` forever if the mutex were ever poisoned, since `try_start_update`'s own gate rejects every future `POST /update/trigger` with 409 while `Applying` — a poisoned mutex would permanently wedge the self-update mechanism, and `GET /update/status` would report "applying" indefinitely even after the task had actually finished, with zero operator-visible signal. Extracted a shared `set_phase()` helper using the identical poison-recovery pattern as `try_start_update` and routed both finish-sites through it, so the whole mutex now has one consistent poison policy instead of two disagreeing ones. Separately, `gemini-code-assist` flagged `hacker_news::algolia_domain_entities` (the T2.24 determinism fix from earlier this arc) for doing more allocation/hashing than necessary: it collected into a `HashSet<String>` purely for deduplication, then converted back to a `Vec` to sort — when a plain `Vec` collect followed by `sort_unstable()` + `dedup()` produces the identical deterministic result with no hashing and one fewer allocation. Applied the suggested rewrite in the same commit; the pre-existing `algolia_domain_entities_emits_all_distinct_domains_deterministically` test passed unmodified, confirming behavioural equivalence. [correctness, code+tests] | A self-update mechanism that can silently wedge itself into a permanent "applying" state — rejecting every future update attempt with no diagnosable error — is exactly the kind of operator-invisible failure mode this project's own conventions call out for closure; catching it via automated PR review before merge, rather than after a real poisoning incident in the field, is the review process working as intended. Folding in the independent efficiency suggestion in the same commit keeps the PR's outstanding review threads resolved together rather than trickling out as separate low-value commits. | +1 test (`set_phase_recovers_from_a_poisoned_mutex`): poisons a real `Mutex` via `catch_unwind` around a panicking lock guard, then asserts `set_phase` still lands the phase transition past the poison (fail-before confirmed: reverted `set_phase`'s body to the bare `if let Ok(...)` pattern in place, the poisoned-mutex assertion failed). Full gate green: fmt/clippy/doc clean, full suite 0 failures (4413 lib tests), architecture suite 30/30. |

The comment showed `.take(8)` was a refactor-preserved bound, not a deliberate DoS cap (unlike AU-037, which documents its truncate caps and prints the totals; the sibling AU-051 has no handle cap). Emit every reachable handle uid (`handle_set` is a `BTreeSet` → sorted/deterministic). [fidelity, no-truncation, correlator, code+tests] | The finding's whole value is "these directly-reachable email/phone identifiers pivot to the associates at this shared residence/line" — silently omitting the 9th+ handle under-reports the linkage a dossier/API consumer reads and truncates the pivot set an analyst uses, with no signal any were dropped. A uid list has no display-size concern (unlike a description), and the sibling AU-051 already emits all, so full fidelity is simply removing the cap. | +1 test (`au049_references_every_reachable_handle_not_a_capped_eight`): two persons + 10 emails at one residence → all 10 handle uids in `entity_uids` (fail-before: 8); the existing AU-049/050 tests still pass; full suite green (4562). **Closes the fidelity-audit arc — all 7 confirmed silent-fidelity violations fixed.** |

Smith`, google `J Smith`), only the first surfaced — the fuller/alternate legal-name variants, genuine identity data, were silently discarded and never stamped on evidence. Now emits one `Person` per DISTINCT name (deterministic `BTreeMap` keyed by lowercased value), tagged with ALL platforms that reported it; identical names dedup to one Person carrying every platform tag. Emitting every distinct variant (deduped, provenance-tagged) is the full-fidelity behaviour the contract requires. | +1 test (`email_emits_a_person_for_each_distinct_reported_name`): facebook+twitter `Jon Smith`, linkedin `Jonathan A. Smith`, github `jsmith` → two distinct `Person`s, the shared name tagged both platforms, the space-less handle excluded (fail-before: one Person); the existing single-name Person test still passes; full suite green (4561) |
| 2026-07-04 | **Full-fidelity — `entities_filtered()` silently capped the filtered browse query at 500 rows; now returns the complete result.** Fidelity-audit-workflow-confirmed. The `GET /scans/{id}/entities/filter` storage query (`storage::entities::entities_filtered`) appended a hardcoded `LIMIT 500`, while the handler returns via `ok_list` with NO limit/offset/total/has_more — so a breach-heavy scan whose filtered result exceeds 500 entities (routine for `kind=email` or a broad `q`) silently dropped the lowest-confidence matches past rank 500, with zero client signal, while the sibling `/entities/facets` still reported the true larger count (an observable inconsistency). The filtered set is a SUBSET of the canonical `entities_for_scan` (which backs `/entities`, CSV, `report.json`, GEXF) and is itself unbounded, so the cap had no memory justification; `confidence DESC, uid ASC` is a total deterministic order. Removed the LIMIT. [fidelity, no-truncation, code+tests] | A browse/filter view that silently returns 500 of 800 matching entities — with the facet count next to it saying 800 — is a direct, self-contradicting truncation of query results the operator relies on to review a scan; the full-fidelity contract requires the complete set (or explicit pagination), and the canonical unfiltered path already proves the full set is served unbounded. Removing an arbitrary cap on a subset of an unbounded set is the minimal fidelity-preserving fix. | +1 test (`entities_filtered_returns_the_complete_result_not_a_capped_500`): seed 600 email entities → 600 returned (fail-before: 500); the four existing entities_filtered tests still pass; full suite green (4560) |

[fidelity, no-dropped-field, code+tests] | An infostealer login is a first-class OSINT identity: on a username scan it is the email the handle maps to (a new pivot the query didn't contain), and on an IP scan it is the compromised account exfiltrated from that victim host — core stealer-log intelligence. | 2026-07-04 | **Dropped-field — DeHashed silently discarded an email mis-stored in the `password` slot; its two breach siblings recover it.** Workflow-confirmed. `dehashed::build`'s password loop guarded on `matches!(classify_credential_field(p), CredentialField::Secret)` only — no `Email` arm — so when the shared classifier (`util::extract::classify_credential_field`, which returns `Email` for a value that `looks_like_email`) flagged an email in the password field (a common stealer/breach quirk), DeHashed minted nothing and the lead was lost. Both siblings recover it: `oathnet_pro::breach` and `see_know::extract` mint it as an `Email` at 0.45 tagged `recovered-from-password` (minting it as a `Password` would forge a reused-secret link across every row with the same quirk). Converted the DeHashed loop to the same three-arm `match` (Sentinel drop / Email recover / Secret mint), single-sourcing the policy so the three breach parsers don't drift. [completeness, no-dropped-lead, single-source, code+tests] | A recovered email is a real subject/associate identifier and a first-class pivot; dropping it because a provider put it in the wrong slot is pure recall loss on a paid breach source, and — worse — the alternative (minting it as a Password) would have forged a false reused-credential link. DeHashed was the one breach parser of three that neither recovered NOR mis-minted it; it simply lost the lead. Sharing the sibling recovery keeps the three parsers' handling of this quirk identical. | +1 test (`email_in_the_password_slot_is_recovered_as_an_email_lead`): an email in `password` → an `Email` tagged `recovered-from-password`, not a `Password` (fail-before: nothing minted); the existing plaintext-password test still passes; full suite green (4555) |
| 2026-07-04 | **Precision — `to_e164_au`'s bare-`61` branch fabricated an AU number from a foreign one; now applies the same ACMA trunk-digit gate as its sibling.** Workflow-confirmed single-source drift. `core::validation::to_e164_au`'s AU-local (`0`+9-digit) branch requires a real ACMA national lead (`matches!(compact[1], 2\|3\|4\|5\|7\|8)`) — added earlier to stop a foreign 10-digit local number being re-typed as `+61…` — but the AU-international-without-`+` (`61`+9-digit) branch gated only on `!nat.starts_with('0')`, so a foreign national number whose lead is `1`/`6`/`9` (a French mobile `0612345678` written `61612345678`) was fabricated into `+61612345678`, a non-existent AU number and its derived mobile/fixed-line/jurisdiction classification. Applied the identical trunk-digit gate to the `61` branch, single-sourcing the AU-lead rule across both inference paths. [precision, no-fabrication, validation, code+tests] | A fabricated phone number is a fabricated identifier an analyst may act on directly (call, subpoena, cross-reference), and here it also fabricates a whole AU line-type + jurisdiction classification off a foreign number — a wrong analytic conclusion. The AU-local branch was already hardened against exactly this; the `61` branch was the same logic missing the same gate, so the two inference paths disagreed on what counts as a valid AU number. | +1 test (`bare_61_prefix_requires_a_real_au_trunk_digit`): leads 1/6/9 rejected (fail-before: `61612345678` → `+61612345678`), leads 2/3/4/5/7/8 still canonicalise; the existing `61412345678` test still passes; full suite green (4554) |
| 2026-07-04 | **Parse-layer precision — the email byte-scanners' local-part class omitted `%`, truncating a `%`-containing mailbox.** Workflow-confirmed single-source drift. Added `%` to both predicates so they match the canonical class. [precision, parse, single-source, code+tests] | A truncated local part is a fabricated identifier: `percent@example.com` is a different mailbox than the real `with%percent@example.com`, and minting it both loses the real address and forges a wrong one that can seed permutations and reuse correlations. A byte-scanner that claims to mirror `EMAIL_RE` must accept the same local class it does; `%` was the one class member both copies dropped. | +2 tests (`page_emails_keeps_a_percent_in_the_local_part`, `email_extraction_keeps_a_percent_in_the_local_part`), each asserting the full `%`-mailbox survives and cross-checking `EMAIL_RE`; full suite green (4553) |
| 2026-07-04 | **Correctness — two cross-scan-history idempotency probes matched a bare substring, silently dropping a genuine distinct link.** Two workflow-confirmed instances of the same bug in `core::engine::history`. `endpoint_has_cooccurrence` keyed idempotency on `ev.summary.contains(partner)` and `endpoint_has_relation_recall` on `contains(kind) && contains(partner)` — unanchored substrings — while the summaries they probe write the partner BACKTICK-delimited (`` Co-occurred with `{partner}` ``) and the kind PAREN-delimited (`(subdomain_of)`). So an entity already bridged to `` `alice2` `` made the probe report it as already linked to a NEW partner `alice` (a substring of `alice2`), so the linker skipped attaching the genuine `alice` co-occurrence/recall evidence — a real cross-investigation association lost whenever one partner (or kind) is a substring of another (numbered handles like `bob`/`bob2`; a relation kind that is a substring of another). Fixed both to match the delimited token the summary actually writes (`` `{partner}` ``, `({kind})`). [correctness, no-dropped-link, engine, code+tests] | Cross-scan co-occurrence and relation-recall are the "bridge investigations" signal — the whole point of the local intelligence database is to surface that this identifier recurred WITH a specific partner in an earlier case. Silently dropping that link because the partner's value happens to be a substring of a previously-recorded one (extremely common with `handle`/`handle2` numbering) is a real recall loss on exactly the high-value cross-case pivot the feature exists to provide, and it is invisible (the link simply never appears). Anchoring on the delimiter the summary already uses makes the probe say precisely what it means. | +1 test (`idempotency_probes_match_the_delimited_partner_token_not_a_substring`): the recorded `alice2`/`bob2` still match (idempotency preserved) but the substrings `alice`/`bob` do not (fail-before: they matched, dropping the real link); the existing idempotency tests still pass; full suite green (4551) |
| 2026-07-04 | **Parse-layer precision — the SeekNow stealer URL gate was weaker than its oathnet_pro twin (a bare `len >= 4`); now single-sourced.** Workflow-confirmed. `see_know::extract::extract_entities` minted the stealer `url`/`url_str` field as a `Url` entity on `url.len() >= 4` alone — no scheme, no host — while the sibling `oathnet_pro::stealer` (whose model see_know's own comment claims to mirror, "exactly OathNet's stealer model") gates the identical field on `u.starts_with("http") && u.contains('.')`. So a native-app URI (`android://…`), a scheme-less fragment, or a capture sentinel ≥4 chars became a bogus `Url` node in see_know that oathnet_pro rejects. Fixed by mirroring the twin's gate (trim, then scheme + dotted host); the paired `<username>@<url>` `Credential` stays ungated, as in oathnet_pro (a login for a native-app surface is still a real credential). [precision, parse, single-source, code+tests] | A phantom `Url` from a stealer log is not a cosmetic dedup issue: a captured login surface is a first-class pivot that seeds crawl / DNS / certificate expansion, so a bogus `android://…` or scheme-less node misdirects that expansion onto nothing (or onto a platform's own infra), wasting the scan and polluting the graph — the exact class the sibling parser's scheme+dot gate exists to prevent. Two stealer consumers claiming to share one model must actually share the gate, or one silently admits what the other rejects. | +1 test (`extract_entities_rejects_non_web_stealer_url_but_keeps_the_credential`): an `android://com.example.app` url mints no `Url` but still yields the login↔surface `Credential`; the existing real-`https://` stealer test still passes; full suite green (4550) |
| 2026-07-04 | **MITRE precision — the five name-less `Social` modules over-claimed ATT&CK T1589.003 (Employee Names); each now maps to its real collection.** Closes the follow-up deferred by the `username_search` fix. `streaming_probe`, `gaming_profile`, `discord_snowflake`, `structured_id`, and `fediverse` are all `ModuleCategory::Social` with no override, so each inherited the default `["T1593.001", "T1589.003"]` — but NONE emits a real-name `Person` (their `produces()` are `Url`/`Username`/`Email`/`MacAddress`), so every finding falsely stamped a gathered person-name. Corrected each: the three handle/platform modules (`streaming_probe`, `gaming_profile`, `discord_snowflake`) → `["T1593.001"]` (Social Media only); `fediverse` → `["T1589.002", "T1593.001"]` (it also emits profile emails, like nostr); `structured_id` → `["T1592.001"]` (Gather Victim Host Information: Hardware) — it's an OFFLINE structured-ID decoder whose signal is the generating machine's MAC address in a UUIDv1, not a social search, so it drops BOTH inherited social techniques. `username_variants` deliberately keeps T1589.003 (a conscious convention) and is untouched. [precision, MITRE, code+tests] | The inline `attack:` tag is HSE's only MITRE surface, so the module→technique map's precision IS the product's ATT&CK fidelity: an analyst reading Reconnaissance coverage off the tags would otherwise mis-file five handle/ID modules as "Employee Names gathered" — asserting a name was obtained on findings that carry none — and would mis-file an offline UUID→MAC host-hardware decode as social-media search. Applying the guard-encoded "emits Person ⟺ claims Employee Names" rule (and honest technique selection for the non-social decoder) to the last five inheritors makes the ATT&CK footprint uniform and truthful. | +7 guard assertions in `attack_overrides_attribute_collection_modules_precisely` (a 3-module loop → `["T1593.001"]` + `!contains T1589.003`; explicit `fediverse` → `["T1589.002","T1593.001"]`; `structured_id` → `["T1592.001"]` + neither T1589.003 nor T1593.001); full suite green (4549) |
| 2026-07-04 | **False-merge precision — AU-042 fused every `pgp-linked` email in the scan into one owner (unpartitioned by key) and fired on a single address; now partitioned by key fingerprint, ≥2 per key.** Workflow-confirmed. `rule_au_042_pgp_email_identity` collected ALL `pgp-linked` `Email` entities scan-wide and emitted ONE High "a PGP key links N emails to one owner" over the whole set, firing whenever the set was non-empty. Two defects: (1) no key-fingerprint partition — emails bound to two DIFFERENT PGP keys (two potentially-different people) were merged into a single asserted owner, a false identity merge; (2) fires on one email — a lone `pgp-linked` address produced a degenerate "links 1 email to one owner" High assertion, though the docstring's contract is "two or more." The `pgp` module already stamps each pgp-linked email with a `key_fingerprint` evidence attribute, so the fix partitions on it: group emails by fingerprint (deterministic `BTreeMap` fingerprint→address→uid), emit one finding PER KEY binding ≥2 distinct addresses, name the fingerprint; an email with several fingerprints belongs to each key it bound, an email with none is excluded. [precision, no-false-merge, correlator, code+tests] | Two PGP keys are two cryptographic identities: merging the emails they bind into one asserted "owner" fabricates a person, cross-attributing one key-holder's addresses to another — the same worst-class false merge AU-046/AU-081 guard against, here at the strongest same-owner tier the engine emits (a key holder's own signed assertion). And a "1 email → one owner" firing is a contentless High that inflates the identity graph with a non-link. Partitioning on the fingerprint the pgp module already provides makes the finding say exactly what the key proves — these addresses, this key — and nothing more. | +2 tests (`au042_does_not_fuse_emails_from_two_distinct_keys`: key A binds two, key B binds two → two findings of two, never one of four — fail-before against the single fused finding; `au042_does_not_fire_for_a_single_pgp_linked_email`: a lone bound address does not fire); the existing group test updated to attach the `key_fingerprint` a real pgp hit always carries; full suite green (4549) |
| 2026-07-04 | **False-merge precision — AU-046 fused EVERY platform-sourced identifier in the scan into every alias; now scoped to the alias's own account.** Workflow-confirmed. `rule_au_046_cross_platform_identity_resolution` built one scan-wide `resolved` set (every `Email`/`Person` with a code/forum/social corroborating source) and attributed ALL of it, at `Severity::High`, to EVERY alias — with no tie between an identifier and the alias's actual account and no role-mailbox gate. So a co-author's email surfaced from a different platform account, a second alias's identifiers, or a `noreply@` support desk were all fused into a person's "resolves to N real-world identifiers" finding — the exact stranger-fusion the docstring falsely claimed it "can't" do. Docstring corrected to describe the real shared-source linkage. [precision, no-false-merge, correlator, code+tests] | A wrong identity resolution is the worst outcome for an evidentiary OSINT tool: it doesn't add a spurious node, it asserts — at High severity, as the subject-as-hub payoff of an alias investigation — that a specific real person owns email/name identifiers that in fact belong to a co-author, another handle, or an org role desk, cross-contaminating two people's dossiers and every conclusion drawn from the merge. | 2026-07-04 | **No-fabrication precision — `streaming_probe` stamped a flat 0.92 confidence and asserted a sensitive identity on every hit, including bare status-only detections.** Workflow-confirmed. The cam/fan-subscription/adult-video prober fans out across ~43 platforms; 41 detect on a bare `Detect::StatusEq(200)` (a HEAD/GET 200 alone) and only 2 on `Detect::StatusAndNotBody` (200 AND the body lacks the platform's "not found" marker). Every `Found` minted a `Url` at a hard-coded 0.92 and any cam/fans/adult hit stamped the summary with `cam-identity-exposed` / `subscription-platform-found` / `adult-profile-found` — but a status-only 200 is weak evidence (a soft-404, a CloudFlare interstitial, or a catch-all route answers 200 for any handle), so a single unverified probe fabricated a high-confidence, reputationally-damaging "this person has a cam/adult identity" claim. Mirrored the sibling `username_search`'s established fix: a pure `detection_strength(detect) → (f64, bool)` returns `(0.92, true)` for a body-verified hit and `(0.74, false)` for a status-only one; each `Url` carries its tiered confidence and a `verified-detection` / `weak-detection` provenance tag; the strong exposure tags are gated on a body-verified hit in that category (weak-only categories still surface their 0.74 URLs — the lead isn't lost, only its unearned assertion); the summary records `hits_verified` / `hits_status_only`. Emit logic extracted to a pure, testable `build_entities`. [precision, no-fabrication, code+tests] | Asserting that a real person operates a webcam/adult/fan-subscription identity is one of the most reputationally-sensitive claims an OSINT tool can emit — fabricating it from an unverified HTTP 200 is precisely the false-positive an evidentiary product must never produce, and doing so at 0.92 confidence would carry it straight into the dossier headline and the correlator's identity fusion. Tiering the confidence and gating the exposure claim on body-verified evidence keeps the lead visible (as a weak, discountable 0.74 URL) while refusing to assert the identity without proof — the same discipline the sibling presence-prober already holds. | +2 tests (`detection_strength_tiers_status_only_below_body_verified`; `build_entities_tiers_confidence_and_gates_exposure_on_verified`: a status-only cam hit → 0.74 + weak-detection + NO cam-identity-exposed, a body-verified hit → 0.92 + verified-detection + the exposure tag); full suite green (4547) |
| 2026-07-04 | **Export integrity — the `/graph.gexf` API export leaked quarantined `candidate` breach-victims as nodes, and every GEXF caller could emit dangling edges (two coupled workflow-confirmed defects, fixed as one).** The web GEXF endpoint (`api::scan_export::scan_export_gexf`) passed `store.entities_for_scan(id)` UNFILTERED to `entities_to_gexf`, so every quarantined `candidate` (a non-subject breach co-occurrence stranger) became a labelled node — leaking a foreign breach-victim list under the subject's scan, which the CSV/`report.json`/CLI exports all strip by default. Naively filtering candidates would have exposed the second, coupled defect: `render_gexf` already dropped candidate NODES but passed the FULL relation set, so a relation to a filtered candidate emitted an `<edge>` referencing an undeclared node id — structurally-invalid GEXF Gephi rejects. Fixed both at their correct layers: (1) `entities_to_gexf` now emits a relation edge only when BOTH endpoints are declared nodes, making "every edge references a declared node" a serializer invariant that protects every caller (CLI and API); (2) `scan_export_gexf` filters `candidate` rows by default with a `?include_candidates=1` opt-in, matching the CSV endpoint. [privacy, export-integrity, no-PII-leak, code+tests] | Leaking a list of unrelated breach victims — real people who merely co-occur in a breach corpus — under a target's scan export is a serious third-party PII disclosure an intelligence tool must never make by default; the whole `candidate` quarantine exists to prevent it, and GEXF was the one export that bypassed it. A structurally-invalid graph that Gephi refuses to open is a silent capability loss for the analyst doing link analysis.
