| 2026-07-04 | **GEOINT precision (C5) — AU-059's class-diversity weight was a global scalar (a mathematical no-op); now per-point.** `au059_synergy_fix` is the single source of the dossier's headline "best AU location estimate" and the API's `best_location` fields. It boosted each coordinate's weight by `class_bonus = 1.0 + (classes.len() - 1) * 0.10` — but `classes.len()` is the SCAN-WIDE distinct-class count, so the same constant multiplied every point's weight. A weighted geometric median (and the centroid fallback) is invariant to scaling all weights by one positive constant, so the "bonus" moved the location estimate **not at all** — while its own comment claimed "a point corroborated across more orthogonal classes pulls proportionally more." Fixed to derive the bonus PER POINT from that entity's own distinct anchoring geo classes (`corroborating_sources` → `geo_source_class`), so a coordinate confirmed by several independent collection methods (e.g. a registry address AND a photo GPS AND a wifi sighting at one spot) genuinely outweighs a single-class sighting and pulls the median toward it. Deterministic (HashSet-len is order-independent); the existing outlier-robustness test (single-class points, bonus 1.0) is unaffected. [precision, GEOINT, no-dead-code, code+tests] | The operator's headline "where is this person" answer should weight a coordinate by how many INDEPENDENT kinds of evidence agree on it — that is the entire premise of the cross-seed synergy rule. A weighting factor that is silently inert means the fix treated a lone IP-geo point and a spot corroborated by three orthogonal methods identically, exactly the failure the rule exists to prevent; and a comment asserting a capability the code doesn't have actively misleads the next reader. Making the documented per-point behaviour real closes both — the same "no dead fields / code must do what it says" doctrine the codebase already enforces. | +1 test (`au059_class_diversity_bonus_is_per_point_not_a_global_no_op`): two scans differing ONLY in the eastern point's class span (3 vs 1), holding its source count / c_effective and every other point fixed — byte-identical fix under the old global scalar (fail-before), strictly east under the per-point bonus (pass-after); full suite green |

It was skipped when its twins were corrected. Added the precise override `["T1593.001"]` (Social Media search only — no bio-email path, so unlike reddit_user no T1589.002) and pinned it in the `attack_overrides_..._precisely` guard, forbidding a regression to the default. Five further un-overridden name-less Social modules (`discord_snowflake`, `fediverse`, `gaming_profile`, `streaming_probe`, `structured_id`) are noted as a discrete follow-up — each emits no `Person` but carries its own technique nuance (fediverse `Email`, structured_id `MacAddress`), so each needs an individual judgement, not a blanket drop. | 2026-07-04 | **Comprehensive 6-subsystem audit — cycle 7/N (final actioned): HTTP response-snippet buffers bound peak memory against a single oversized chunk.** `util::http::fetch`'s `error_snippet` and `read_body_capped` both `extend_from_slice`'d the WHOLE streamed chunk and truncated *afterwards*, so the cap only bounded the buffer after a chunk was already fully copied into RAM. A hostile/misconfigured upstream returning one multi-GB chunk (HTTP/1.1 chunked encoding permits any chunk size) is copied whole before the truncate — an OOM risk on a low-RAM Termux device, worst under the `username_search` 32-way probe fan-out. Extracted a shared pure `append_capped(buf, chunk, cap)` that copies at most `cap - buf.len()` bytes and signals when the cap is hit; both readers now use it, single-sourcing the bound. [robustness, memory-safety, code+tests] | An on-device intelligence tool must be resilient to the very upstreams it queries — a memory-exhaustion vector triggerable by any server the operator scans is a denial-of-service on the operator's own phone mid-operation. Making the cap hold at the chunk boundary (not after) closes it, and single-sourcing the two copies of the streaming-read loop stops them drifting. Small, defence-in-depth, but exactly the "never trust a hostile upstream to be well-behaved" discipline the redaction/SSRF/size-cap layer already embodies. | +3 tests (`append_capped_bounds_a_single_oversized_chunk_to_the_cap`, `..._accumulates_small_chunks_until_the_cap`, `..._is_a_noop_once_already_at_cap`); full suite green. **Closes the comprehensive audit's actioned backlog — 12 repairs across 7 cycles.** |
| 2026-07-04 | **Comprehensive 6-subsystem audit — cycle 6/N: determinism + concurrency correctness — a stable Netlas JARM and a single-probe circuit breaker.** Two independent defects. **(1) Determinism.** `modules/netlas::build_entities` accumulated a host's JARM fingerprints in a `HashSet` and surfaced one via `jarm_seen.iter().next()` — but `HashSet` order is randomised per process (SipHash, random seed), so a host exposing several JARMs emitted a DIFFERENT `jarm_fingerprint` between otherwise-identical runs, breaking byte-identical output. Switched to `BTreeSet` → the lexicographically smallest fingerprint, deterministically. **(2) Concurrency.** `util::circuit_breaker::Breaker::allow` matched `Closed | HalfOpen => true`, so once an open host's cooldown elapsed and the first caller flipped it to `HalfOpen`, EVERY concurrent caller in that window also got `true` — a thundering herd on a host that is very likely still down, defeating the single-trial-probe design. `HalfOpen` now denies concurrent callers (exactly one probe in flight); `retry_at` doubles as the probe deadline (via a new `enter_half_open`), so a probe whose outcome is never recorded self-heals into a fresh probe one cooldown later instead of wedging the breaker. [determinism, concurrency, code+tests] | Byte-identical output is a core invariant of an evidentiary tool — two runs of the same scan must produce the same dossier/export, or a reviewer can't tell a real change from serialization noise; a process-randomised JARM silently violated it. And a recovery breaker that admits a herd instead of one probe re-hammers a rate-limited/downed host exactly when it's most fragile, burning the mobile-radio budget the breaker exists to protect. Both restore invariants the codebase already holds elsewhere (deterministic ordered collections; the engine breaker's one-trip discipline). | +2 tests (`build_entities_emits_a_deterministic_jarm_fingerprint`: 3 out-of-order JARMs → the smallest; `half_open_admits_exactly_one_probe_and_self_heals_a_lost_outcome`) + the existing `after_cooldown_allows_once_and_goes_half_open` updated to assert the 2nd concurrent caller is denied; full suite green |

| 2026-07-04 | **Comprehensive 6-subsystem audit — cycle 4/N: engine finalise/dispatch robustness — a panicking rule can't abort a scan, and a cache replay can't reset the circuit breaker.** Two robustness gaps in the same finalise/dispatch flow, both making a scan degrade gracefully rather than silently lose work. **(1) Panic containment parity.** The live incremental correlation pass already wraps `correlate_entities` in `catch_unwind`, but the authoritative finalise-time `Correlator::run` ran UNGUARDED — so a rule panicking on adversarial persisted data (a slice-index bug over a crafted entity) would unwind the entire finalise block, losing the terminal `ScanComplete` event AND the API-key pool the scan harvested (the key-pool save, WAL checkpoint, and events-prune all run after the correlator in `finalise_scan`). A `from_cache` flag makes replays invisible to the breaker (neither success nor failure); the 3 cache-hit call sites pass `true`, the 3 real-dispatch sites `false`. [robustness, engine, code+tests] | An OSINT tool an agency runs unattended must never lose a completed scan's intel to a single bad rule, nor let stale cached data mask a provider that has actually died mid-run — both are silent failures that corrupt the operator's picture (a lost dossier reads as "nothing found"; a masked-dead provider reads as "still healthy" while it burns dispatch slots the working sources need). | 2026-07-04 | **Comprehensive 6-subsystem audit — cycle 2/N: serve-layer hardening — CSRF on every mutating endpoint, loopback-only debug logs, and a real autonomous-sweep de-dup.** Web/API audit surfaced three defects. A `enforce_csrf` middleware now requires an `X-HSE-CSRF` header on every mutating method (`POST`/`PUT`/`DELETE`/`PATCH`) across `/api`; the SPA injects it transparently via a global `fetch` wrapper and CLI/API clients send `-H 'X-HSE-CSRF: 1'`. (2) **Debug logs:** `GET /api/v1/logs` streams the TRACE ring buffer (scan targets + discovered PII) but lacked the `is_loopback()` peer gate its sibling key-pool/settings endpoints carry — added via `ConnectInfo`, so a LAN bind no longer serves it. (3) `scan_auto_sweep`'s per-target de-dup keyed on the per-call-unique `scan_id` (a silent no-op — nothing was ever skipped), so one sweep spawned duplicate scans for a target reachable by several search engines; it now de-dups on the `(kind, value)` target identity. Serving the raw TRACE log (which contains the very PII the tool exists to find) to any LAN peer is a direct subject-data leak. | 2026-06-24 | **`comb_search` — free, keyless emulation of SeekNow's stealer/credential corpus (real public source, no API).** New free Breach module (pri 129) querying ProxyNova's public COMB index (`api.proxynova.com/comb`) — genuine leaked `identity:secret` lines, no key, no mock. Accepts Email/Username/Domain. The correctness core is [`line_matches_target`]: COMB matches the query as a **substring** (a query for `qwerty-zzz@nope.invalid` returns `qwerty-zzz@bk.ru:…`), so every returned line is strictly post-filtered to the EXACT identity (full email / exact host / exact local-part) before any entity is minted; username matches are candidate-quarantined; secret-echoes (`user@x:user@x`) and capture sentinels (`[fail]`) are dropped via `classify_credential_field`. Live integration test (ignored) verified end-to-end against the real endpoint: 79 entities for `example.com` (75 exact-host accounts + 3 passwords), zero stranger attribution. The paid `see_know` API path is untouched (kept as the optional, key-gated counterpart). The provider's own free frontend (`see-know.icu`) sits behind a Cloudflare JS challenge — solving it would be brittle evasion and is correctly out of scope; so the emulation uses a real, keyless public breach index instead. The substring-exactness filter is what makes this safe for an LE/defence dossier: it never attributes a stranger's password to the subject (the exact failure a naïve COMB wrapper would commit). Free-tier, offline-deterministic parsing, single bounded request — Termux-safe. | 3789 ✅ |
